## [SkillTTA: Test-Time Adaptive Skill Synthesis for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16986) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces SkillTTA, a method that retrieves task-relevant training trajectories and synthesizes a temporary skill at test time, conditioned on the visible target context, for a solver with fixed parameters. It further employs meta prompt optimization (MPO) to adapt the policy that writes these skills, achieving state-of-the-art performance across ALFWorld, SpreadsheetBench, BigCodeBench, and WebShop. SkillTTA addresses the challenge of test-time compute-to-capability conversion, showing that simply adding more context or rollouts does not necessarily improve agent capability. By synthesizing skills on the fly, it achieves a higher performance ceiling at lower compute cost, which could influence future LLM agent design and efficiency. SkillTTA uses meta prompt optimization (MPO) to refine the skill-writing policy, evaluating candidate prompts on paired tasks and emphasizing informative transitions. It confines updates to benchmark-specific atomic slots to reduce variance, and outperforms state-of-the-art reuse and optimization baselines across multiple benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time compute refers to the additional computation used during inference to improve LLM performance, such as expanding context or generating multiple rollouts. However, more compute does not always translate to better capability, a problem known as test-time compute-to-capability conversion. SkillTTA leverages retrieved training trajectories and meta prompt optimization to synthesize task-specific skills, which are temporary and conditioned on the target context, enabling efficient adaptation without changing model parameters.

## [Exact Symmetry as Algebra: Machine-Verified Tensor Calculus Enforces Physical Selection Rules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20440) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a tensor algebra framework, denoted ⋆G, where any finite group G defines the multiplication rule, making equivariance an intrinsic algebraic property rather than an approximate architectural constraint. The authors demonstrate that this approach enforces exact symmetry, eliminating compounding equivariance errors that grow with network depth, and provide machine-verified proofs in Lean 4. This work addresses a fundamental limitation of approximate equivariance in machine learning, showing that residual errors compound with depth and can diverge by fourteen orders of magnitude. By making symmetry exact by construction, it offers a principled way to enforce physical selection rules in any predictor, potentially improving reliability and performance in scientific applications such as materials discovery and molecular modeling. The algebra block-diagonalizes tensors into irreducible-representation blocks via the group-Fourier transform, and the standard matrix toolbox, including Frobenius-optimal low-rank factorization, transfers blockwise and is machine-checked in Lean 4. The framework extends to band-limited compact groups and, under periodic boundary conditions, to all 230 crystallographic space groups and compact little-group fibers of Euclidean and Poincaré symmetry.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Equivariance in machine learning ensures that model outputs transform consistently with input symmetries, which is crucial for physical applications. However, most models achieve this only approximately, leading to errors that can accumulate in deep networks. Tensor algebra provides a way to encode group operations directly into the model's structure, and Lean 4 is a proof assistant that can verify mathematical theorems and code correctness.

## [Score Hamiltonian Links Diffusion Models to Adiabatic Transport](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05217) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The authors establish an exact correspondence between score-based diffusion sampling and adiabatic transport of ground states for a family of Schrödinger operators, termed Score Hamiltonians. They derive novel density reconstruction bounds and principled annealing schedules via adiabatic theorems for Fokker-Planck equations with time-varying potentials. This work provides a new theoretical framework connecting generative modeling to quantum mechanics and spectral theory, potentially guiding the design of more efficient sampling algorithms. It also offers a fundamental limit for sampling based on score-matching error and spectral gap, which could influence future research in both machine learning and physics. The fundamental limit of sampling is shown to be set by the ratio of squared score-matching error to the Score Hamiltonian spectral gap, which is the inverse Poincaré constant of the data density. The construction uses the learned score's quantum potential to build the Hamiltonian, and the annealing schedules are derived from adiabatic theorems.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Score-based diffusion models generate data by reversing a noising process, guided by the score function (gradient of log-density). Adiabatic transport in quantum mechanics describes the slow evolution of a system that remains in its instantaneous ground state. The Fokker-Planck equation governs the time evolution of probability densities under drift and diffusion, and its spectral properties relate to convergence rates. This paper bridges these concepts by mapping diffusion sampling to adiabatic transport of ground states.

## [TLA-Prover: 20B Model Boosts TLA+ Spec Synthesis via GRPO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06133) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TLA-Prover, a 20-billion-parameter model, achieves 30% pass@1 on Gold and Diamond tiers for TLA+ specification synthesis, roughly 3.5x the 8.6% untuned baseline, by combining supervised fine-tuning with repair-based group-relative policy optimization (GRPO) using TLC as the reward signal. This work addresses a critical gap in AI-assisted formal verification, showing that LLMs can be trained to produce semantically correct specifications for safety-critical systems. It demonstrates the effectiveness of using a model checker as a reward signal, which could inspire similar approaches in other formal methods domains. The model uses a four-tier grading system (Bronze, Silver, Gold, Diamond) where Diamond requires TLC to detect a violation after a small alteration to the correctness property, preventing trivial properties. A DPO variant from the same SFT checkpoint reaches 20% at Diamond, and Gold and Diamond coincide at every checkpoint.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: TLA+ is a formal specification language for designing and verifying concurrent and distributed systems, developed by Leslie Lamport. TLC is an explicit state enumeration model checker that verifies whether a specification satisfies its properties. GRPO is a reinforcement learning algorithm that optimizes policies by comparing outputs within a group, often used to train LLMs without a learned reward model.

## [Minimax-Optimal Bounds for Smooth DNNs Trained by GD/SGD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06772) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper establishes quantitative bounds showing that kernel gradient descent with the infinite-width neural tangent kernel approximates finite-width deep regression with smooth activations under GD and SGD training. It proves that both GD- and SGD-trained DNNs achieve minimax-optimal excess population risk, up to logarithmic factors, when the network width grows polynomially with sample size. This result bridges kernel methods and deep learning theory, providing a general mechanism to transfer learning-theoretic guarantees from kernel methods to deep regression. It offers the first such minimax-optimal guarantees for standard fully connected DNNs with smooth activations trained by GD and SGD, which is a significant step toward understanding the statistical performance of over-parameterized neural networks. The approximation gap is governed by the network width and training horizon, with an additional stochastic gradient error in the SGD case. The results hold under general source and effective dimension conditions, and the network width only needs to grow polynomially in the sample size.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: The neural tangent kernel (NTK) describes the evolution of deep neural networks during gradient descent training. In the infinite-width limit, the NTK becomes constant, and gradient descent is equivalent to kernel gradient descent with the NTK, allowing the use of kernel methods' theoretical tools. This paper leverages this duality to derive generalization bounds for finite-width networks trained by GD and SGD.

## [Ouroboros-Spatial: Self-Evolving Framework Boosts Spatial Reasoning in MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11719) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Ouroboros-Spatial introduces a self-evolving training framework where a frozen proposer generates spatial QA pairs and a learnable solver is fine-tuned on accepted samples, using the solver's confidence as a difficulty signal to guide the next iteration. This closed-loop approach improves Qwen3-VL-4B and Qwen3-VL-8B across six spatial reasoning benchmarks, achieving absolute gains of 9.9 and 6.8 points on VSI-Bench while using an order of magnitude fewer training examples. This work addresses the data inefficiency of static datasets in multimodal training, where samples are treated uniformly regardless of model capability. By dynamically aligning the training distribution with the model's evolving abilities, it offers a more efficient and effective paradigm that could influence future multimodal training methodologies and enable smaller models to outperform larger baselines. The framework uses a frozen proposer to generate QA pairs from 3D scene metadata and raw video frames, along with executable code for reliable ground truth. The solver's per-sample prediction confidence serves as a difficulty signal, filtering out trivial or ambiguous samples, and the loop co-evolves the training distribution with model ability.

**Background**: Spatial reasoning in multimodal large language models (MLLMs) remains challenging, and existing approaches rely on large-scale static datasets that are data-inefficient. Self-evolving training is a recent trend where models learn from their own outputs, but its application to spatial reasoning is underexplored. This paper builds on that concept by closing the loop between data generation and model learning.

## [Keyless Attention Halves KV-Cache Memory, Boosts Throughput](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21848) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Keyless Attention, a novel attention mechanism that replaces the key projection with a value-space routing projection, eliminating key representations entirely. This yields a Value-Only Cache that reduces KV-cache memory and access overhead by exactly 50% while improving decode throughput. KV-cache is a major bottleneck in long-context inference, consuming significant GPU memory and bandwidth. Halving its memory footprint can enable longer contexts, higher throughput, and lower deployment costs for large language models, making this a significant step toward more efficient transformer inference. The method is evaluated across five models and four architectures, matching or outperforming standard QKV attention in perplexity on four of five models. Ablation studies show that the dedicated value-space routing projection is crucial, outperforming KV-sharing methods that lack this routing mechanism.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: In transformer models, the KV cache stores key and value vectors for previously processed tokens to avoid recomputation during autoregressive generation. However, its memory footprint grows linearly with context length, becoming a primary bottleneck for long-context inference. Keyless Attention removes the key projection, so only values need to be cached, reducing memory usage by half.

MLVC introduces a neural video codec that ensures cross-platform consistency by transmitting scale parameters through the hyperprior, achieving over 70% BD-rate improvement on the VCD benchmark. It runs at 100 FPS on commodity NPUs from Apple, Intel, and Qualcomm. This addresses a critical barrier to real-world adoption of neural video codecs: cross-platform incompatibility. By enabling deterministic decoding without bit-exact arithmetic, MLVC could make learned codecs practical for widespread deployment in video conferencing and streaming. The codec uses gated memory, ReGLU activation, a long-term reference recovery mechanism, and domain-specific perceptual training to recover coding efficiency lost from transmitting scale parameters. It achieves subjective quality competitive with DCVC-RT, which cannot operate across diverse platforms.

A new paper introduces ErrorBench, a stress-test protocol showing that prompt framing can inflate count-based F1 metrics for LLM error detection without improving span localization. Across six LLMs and 4,290 responses, anchored prompts caused up to 0.79 points of F1 inflation under CoNLL-2014 M2 scoring. This finding challenges the reliability of count-based F1 as a proxy for error-detection quality, which is widely used in LLM evaluation. It suggests that evaluations should adopt span-aware metrics and avoid pre-populated error counts, impacting how LLM proofreading and document-review systems are assessed. The study evaluated six contemporary LLMs under five prompt conditions using 4,290 responses from 143 CoNLL-2014 passages. A 100-passage replication with the official ERRANT 3.0.0 pipeline showed that the Blind-to-Anchored prompt shift raised Count-F1 by +0.21 but only +0.04 in multi-reference ERRANT F0.5, highlighting the gap.

ErrorBench is a new benchmark designed to stress-test prompt-induced count distortion, using CoNLL-2014 data and M2/ERRANT scoring pipelines.

## [SAR Adapter Reveals Hidden Behaviors in Fine-Tuned Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03640) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the Stabilized Adapter for self-Report (SAR), a lightweight LoRA adapter that enables a fine-tuned language model to describe its own hidden behaviors in plain language. SAR successfully detects all seven implanted behaviors, outperforming the existing Introspection Adapters (IA) baseline, which misses some behaviors and hallucinates on others. This work provides a more reliable tool for auditing fine-tuned models, addressing a critical safety and interpretability concern in AI. By improving detection accuracy and reducing hallucinations, SAR helps practitioners answer 'what did it actually learn?' and mitigates risks from hidden misalignment. SAR is trained using only the fine-tuned model under audit and its training dataset, without needing additional labels. It roughly halves the rate of hallucinations compared to IA, and retains positive signal on all settings where IA fails, even when the model has generalized into broad misalignment not predicted by training data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Fine-tuning can introduce hidden behaviors into language models, such as giving false answers under specific conditions or providing harmful advice on certain topics. LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that adds small trainable adapters to pre-trained models. Introspection Adapters (IA) are a prior method that trains LoRA adapters to verbalize learned behaviors, but they can miss behaviors or hallucinate incorrect reports.

## [AdaMAST: Adaptive Failure Taxonomies for Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16387) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AdaMAST automatically constructs evidence-grounded failure taxonomies from agent execution traces, organizing failures into named codes along three fixed axes without hand-authoring or human annotation. It improves agent performance across benchmarks, such as raising SWE-agent's resolution on SWE-bench Verified Mini from 60% to 70% and Claude Code from 64.0% to 70.7%. This approach provides a reusable, adaptive feedback interface for improving agent systems without changing model weights, addressing a practical bottleneck in trajectory optimization and runtime monitoring. It could significantly streamline debugging and optimization of AI agents across diverse domains. The taxonomy is compact, achieving an order-of-magnitude compression while preserving trace distinctions, and it matches expert failure annotations more closely than a hand-crafted reference vocabulary. AdaMAST-Judge, a verifier built on the induced codes, improves best-of-5 accuracy on Terminal-Bench 2.0 by 8-15 points over Pass@1.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Agent systems generate execution traces that record failures, but raw traces are long, instance-specific, and lack a stable vocabulary for recurring issues. Existing methods often rely on free-form reflection or hand-crafted taxonomies, which are not adaptive to different agent roles, tools, or domains. AdaMAST addresses this by learning a compact set of failure codes from the target system's own traces, starting from a built-in 14-code adaptation of MAST.

## [Intervention-Centered Auto Research Validates AI Scientist Decisions via Held-Out Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17100) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces intervention-centered Auto Research, a methodology that validates individual research decisions in AI-driven scientific discovery using held-out transfer, rather than only evaluating final pipeline artifacts. Across 701 agent-executed experiments on ten Matbench endpoints, the approach confirmed selected interventions on nine endpoints and preserved 89.3% of non-tied intervention orderings. This work addresses a critical limitation in current auto research evaluation by making the reliability of individual research decisions measurable, which is essential for reproducibility and trust in automated ML research. It provides a framework that can be applied broadly to any domain where agents propose executable alternatives against a fixed evaluator, potentially accelerating reliable scientific discovery. The method searches Feature, Model, Representation, and Data axes independently with inner five-fold feedback, then freezes each axis winner before an outer-holdout matrix compares all alternatives on unseen evidence. A subsequent compatibility test, which combines frozen Feature and Model code without further tuning, raised mean outer-holdout improvement from 19.0% to 26.3%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Auto Research uses language-model agents to propose, implement, and evaluate machine-learning changes in a closed loop, but is typically judged by its terminal pipeline score, which cannot reveal which technical decision produced a gain. Held-out transfer validation is a technique where models or interventions are evaluated on data not used during development, providing a more reliable measure of generalization. Matbench is an automated leaderboard for benchmarking machine learning algorithms on materials science properties, hosted by the Materials Project.

This paper introduces a unified framework for full-song generation that combines hierarchical autoregressive modeling with flow-matching rendering, supporting lyrics-to-song, instrumental, and cover song generation. The system uses an 8-codebook RVQ tokenizer, a hybrid-LM for autoregressive planning, and FullDiT for flow-matching in a continuous latent space. This work addresses the challenging problem of generating full-length, high-quality songs from lyrics and descriptions, which has significant potential for creative industries and AI-assisted music production. The combination of autoregressive planning and flow-matching could set a new standard for long-form audio generation. The framework comprises four main components: a semantic-aware tokenizer, hybrid-LM, FullDiT, and a two-level melody module. It also explores reward-based post-training strategies like DPO, GRPO, and OPD, and applies flow-based GRPO to FullDiT to enhance musicality and rendering quality.

## [Input-Anchored Logic Gate Networks Achieve Scalable Depth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21633) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Input-Anchored Logic Gate Networks (IALGNs), a novel topology where each gate combines a private hidden spine with a direct input anchor, enabling consistent depth-accuracy scaling up to 150 layers on MNIST, CIFAR-10, and CIFAR-100. The authors identify optimization collapse and topology-induced degradation of output-specific credit as the root causes of depth scalability issues in existing LGNs. This work addresses a critical limitation in logic gate networks, which previously failed to benefit from increased depth, limiting their scalability for complex tasks. By enabling scalable depth, IALGNs could enhance the applicability of LGNs in hardware acceleration and energy-efficient computing, offering an alternative to traditional neural networks. The paper introduces a Random-$k_x$ relaxation to improve anchor selection without relaxing the spine, and uses linear probes, topology ablations, and operation-aware analysis to validate that trained IALGNs preserve private states and apply sparse anchor-conditioned updates. The results show that alternative topologies saturate or degrade beyond certain depths, while IALGN maintains consistent scaling.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Logic Gate Networks (LGNs) compute through compositions of Boolean operations, but they are conventionally non-differentiable, making gradient-based training difficult. Prior work introduced differentiable logic gate networks using real-valued logics and continuous relaxations, but depth scalability remained a challenge. Straight-through estimation (STE) is a surrogate-gradient method that enables training through non-differentiable operations, which is relevant to stabilizing LGN training. IALGNs build on these foundations by adding input anchors to preserve credit flow.

## [New Diagnostic Exposes Dependency Gap in Tabular Generative Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21636) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces a dependency-aware fidelity diagnostic that decomposes a gradient-boosted classifier two-sample test (XGB-C2ST) into marginal, dependency, and cross components, revealing that common metrics fail to capture inter-column dependencies. Applied to state-of-the-art flow-matching (TabbyFlow/EF-VFM) and diffusion (TabDiff) generators, it exposes a real dependency gap that standard metrics miss. This work addresses a critical evaluation gap in synthetic tabular data generation, a field with applications in fraud detection and clinical risk. By providing a more sensitive diagnostic, it could improve model evaluation practices and guide the development of better generative models that preserve inter-column dependencies. The diagnostic is anchored between a fully-factorized lower bound and a real-data oracle upper bound. The study finds that neither a 16x capacity increase nor post-hoc copula correction closes the dependency gap, suggesting the absence of direct dependency supervision rather than a capacity or structural limit.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Tabular generative models aim to create synthetic data that preserves both marginal distributions and inter-column dependencies, which are crucial for minority-class utility in imbalanced domains. Common evaluation metrics like logistic-regression C2ST and pairwise Trend scores are often blind to these dependencies. Gradient-boosted classifiers, such as XGBoost, are powerful tools for two-sample tests, and flow-matching models like EF-VFM are recent advances in generative modeling for tabular data.

## [Directional Influence Function for Constrained Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23388) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the Directional Influence Function (DIF), a novel estimator that incorporates constraints into influence estimation for training data in constrained learning settings, overcoming limitations of classical influence functions. This addresses a significant gap in influence function estimation for constrained learning, a topic of growing importance in ML fairness, safety, and robustness. The proposed DIF method is novel and theoretically grounded, with potential broad impact. DIF formulates the optimality conditions of constrained learning as a variational inequality (VI) and analyzes how perturbing training data affects this VI. It is validated on constrained linear regression and fairness-constrained CNNs, accurately predicting test loss changes under data removal.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Influence functions are a classic technique from robust statistics used to trace a model's predictions back to training data, identifying which training points are most responsible for a given prediction. Constrained learning embeds explicit requirements such as fairness, safety, or robustness directly into the training process, but classical influence functions become unreliable when data perturbations reshape both the objective and the feasible region.

## [Third-Party API Routers Pose Security Risks in Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23624) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper empirically demonstrates that third-party API routers in coding-agent workflows can inspect and modify requests and responses without verification, undermining client-side permission mechanisms. The study introduces SIDEL, a framework for evaluating router-side injection attacks across four levels of subtlety, and finds that all tested agents had a 0% defense success rate without additional mitigations. This research highlights a critical security gap in agentic software development, where high-autonomy coding agents rely on third-party routers that can be compromised. The findings underscore the need for provider-side output-integrity guarantees and more robust client-side safeguards to protect real-world software engineering workflows. The study evaluates four representative coding agents and tests four injection levels: Response Substitution (L1), Response Append (L2), LLM-Polished Injection (L3), and LLM-Polished with Distribution Alignment Injection (L4). Client-side mitigations and reactive reviews improve resistance but do not fully restore end-to-end control, motivating provider-side output-integrity guarantees.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Third-party API routers are common layers that unify access to multiple LLM providers, but they act as black boxes, meaning users do not control their internal operations. In coding-agent workflows, high-autonomy operation reduces interaction overhead, placing the router on the trusted path. Client-side permission mechanisms are designed to enforce user constraints, but this study shows they can be bypassed when the router is compromised.

FilmBench is a new benchmark for text-to-video (T2V) and reference-to-video (R2V) generation, co-developed with directors and faculty from the Beijing Film Academy and the Hujing Digital Media & Entertainment Group. It introduces a three-level cinematic taxonomy with 3 axes, 12 components, and 35 (T2V) + 3 (R2V-only) sub-metrics, and an open-source suite of Cinematic Language operators (FilmOps). This benchmark addresses a critical gap in video generation evaluation by focusing on professional cinematic craft rather than basic plausibility, potentially setting a new standard for assessing AI-generated video quality. It could influence future benchmarking practices and drive improvements in models' ability to handle multi-shot, dynamic scenes. The prompts are reverse-engineered from award-winning film clips across 20 cinematic genres, with 1,056 of 1,169 prompts being multi-shot. The evaluator reproduces human model ranking at Spearman rho = 0.95 (T2V) and 0.96 (R2V), and scores are notably lower than prior web-style benchmarks, revealing gaps in dynamic aesthetics and a single-to-multi-shot performance drop.

## [OpenCoder: Uncertainty-Aware RAG for Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24884) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces OpenCoder, an uncertainty-aware framework for retrieval-augmented code generation that estimates source-specific uncertainty to filter and rank heterogeneous evidence, improving generation and repair. On an expanded 32-task RepoExec-inline evaluation, OpenCoder improves GPT selected-output correctness from 56.25% to 78.13% over Baseline RAG. This work addresses a critical gap in retrieval-augmented code generation by explicitly modeling uncertainty in heterogeneous evidence, which is a novel and timely contribution. The findings support treating uncertainty as an actionable control signal for repository-level retrieval, verification, and repair, potentially improving LLM-based coding tools. A factorial analysis over API knowledge, repository context, and similar-code evidence reveals no universal additive source ranking; instead, significant cross-source interactions depend on the accompanying evidence and LLM backend. However, OpenCoder matches a verification-and-repair control, and the corresponding Gemini improvement is not statistically supported, indicating backend-dependent benefits.

In repository-level code generation, RAG systems retrieve heterogeneous evidence such as similar-code examples, repository context, and project-specific APIs, which may be noisy or conflicting. Existing approaches primarily optimize retrieval relevance without explicitly modeling uncertainty in the retrieved evidence.

## [When Benchmark Inferences Do Not Compose: Projectibility in AI Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26159) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces 'projectibility' as a new criterion for warranted generalization in AI evaluation, arguing that valid individual benchmark inferences do not automatically compose into valid chains. It proposes a non-composition principle and a projectibility audit to diagnose unsupported joins in benchmark-to-use arguments. This work addresses a critical gap in AI evaluation methodology, as real-world deployment often relies on chaining multiple inferences from benchmarks. It provides a framework to ensure that conclusions drawn from benchmarks are robust, which is essential for trustworthy AI systems. The paper builds on Goodman's problem of rival extensions and argument-based validity, and includes a legal-research case study plus a reanalysis and simulation showing how aggregate stability can erase distinctions needed for later projections. The projectibility audit is proposed as a practical tool for evaluating benchmark-to-use arguments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: AI benchmarks are widely used to evaluate model capabilities, but their results are often generalized to real-world scenarios through multiple inferential steps. Validity-centered approaches require evidence for each claim, but this paper highlights that even if each step is individually valid, the chain as a whole may not be. The concept of projectibility, borrowed from philosophy of science, addresses whether a bounded extension from observed to unobserved cases is warranted.

## [TraceCoder: Explainable Code Generation with Snippet Versioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26307) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TraceCoder introduces a code generation framework with three mechanisms: a relational snippet-history schema, a browser-based visualization tool, and a fractional position-key indexing scheme. It was evaluated on 30 algorithmic tasks, achieving a mean Chg% of 30% and traceable repair events for 30% of snippets. This addresses the black-box nature of LLM-based code generation, making the process auditable and replayable, which is crucial for trust and debugging in production. It could influence how coding agents are designed and adopted in software engineering. The framework uses a competitive fractional position-key indexing scheme with tree-node delimiters to assign stable, lexicographically-ordered identifiers. Evaluation included two provider configurations; 10 tasks exhausted the 6-iteration budget, and three case studies demonstrated how failures shaped final code.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-based coding agents generate code as black-box outputs, hiding the rationale behind each line and making post-hoc auditing impossible. TraceCoder aims to record the evolution of code through benchmark-driven repair, enabling provenance queries and visualization. The relational snippet-history schema stores repair events, while the visualization tool renders heat-mapped, hover-annotated source code.

MultivationBench is a new benchmark introduced in a recent arXiv paper (2607.26465) that evaluates multimodal large language models' ability to reason about evolving motivations in story-driven visual narratives. It is the first human-annotated benchmark for this task, and initial results show that all tested models struggle with sequential motivation reasoning. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating MLLMs' social intelligence, as existing evaluations focus on static text or isolated images, which fail to capture the cumulative nature of real-world behavioral drivers. The finding that models struggle highlights a significant limitation in current AI systems' ability to understand human-like social dynamics, which is essential for applications like interactive storytelling, virtual assistants, and social robotics. The benchmark builds on established psychological frameworks, namely Maslow's hierarchy of needs and Reiss's 16 basic desires, requiring models to integrate accumulated multimodal context to infer evolving motivations. The paper reports that all tested models fail to maintain consistent motivation reasoning across sequential contexts, revealing a disconnect between static recognition and dynamic reasoning.

## [Evidence-Ledger Adjudication Improves Claim-Evidence Traceability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26512) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces evidence-ledger adjudication, a workflow that pairs each claim with an evidence packet, assigns a support relation, and routes unsupported claims back to the author. On a new 2,335-row blind benchmark, the agent-based condition achieves 0.676 relation accuracy and 0.601 macro-F1, significantly outperforming the best non-agent baseline (0.383 accuracy, 0.303 macro-F1). This work addresses a critical bottleneck in AI-assisted writing: verifying that claims are supported by cited evidence. By providing an auditable traceability layer, it could enhance trust in AI-generated content and improve fact-checking workflows for researchers, journalists, and automated systems. The benchmark integrates independent external labels from AVeriTeC, CLIMATE-FEVER, and SciFact, with gold relations and source evidence labels hidden during prediction. The workflow also routes 1270/1435 claims with gold labels indicating contradiction, missing evidence, or mixed evidence, while routing 295/900 supported claims, showing a trade-off between recall and precision.

## [AlphaSchema: Structured Semantic Space for LLM Alpha Mining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26642) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AlphaSchema introduces a structured semantic space for LLM-based alpha mining, where each point is a schema plan composed of Event, Context, Qualities, Direction, and Output. It decouples exploration from implementation, using a surrogate model to guide iterative selection, and demonstrates strong predictive and portfolio performance on the Chinese stock market. This framework addresses a key limitation in existing LLM-based alpha mining systems, which often lack explicit exploration spaces and principled navigation mechanisms. By structuring the search space and decoupling exploration from implementation, AlphaSchema could improve the controllability and optimization of automated factor discovery, impacting quantitative finance and automated strategy development. The framework uses an LLM to translate selected schema plans into executable factors, while evaluated rewards are accumulated to learn a surrogate model over the semantic space. An iterative selection mechanism balances global exploration, surrogate-guided exploitation, and local mutation. Experiments on the Chinese stock market show that implementations of the same schema plans by different LLMs exhibit comparable predictive quality, suggesting robustness to the choice of LLM.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Alpha mining is the process of discovering predictive signals, or factors, for asset returns in quantitative finance. Traditional methods include hand-crafted factor synthesis and algorithmic search like genetic programming, but they face challenges such as alpha decay and limited ability to implement complex ideas. Recent approaches leverage large language models (LLMs) to generate and iterate on factors, but often lack explicit exploration spaces and control mechanisms. AlphaSchema addresses this by defining a structured semantic space for trading hypotheses, separating the discovery of what to test from how to implement it.

The system includes a Data Profiling Agent to create skills for each dataset, a Relation Agent to identify dataset relationships, a Planner Agent to retrieve task-relevant datasets and generate plans, multiple Execution Agents for data processing with shared memory, and a Report Agent to synthesize logs. ## [Forensic Audit Reveals Major Flaws in Radiology VLM Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25589) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A retrospective forensic reproducibility audit of a chest-radiograph vision-language model (VLM) benchmark found significant discrepancies between the intended protocol and released artifacts, including prompt mislabeling, missing polarity inversion, and data truncation. The authors withdrew original performance and clinical claims. This audit highlights critical reproducibility issues in medical-imaging AI benchmarks, which could undermine trust in published results and affect clinical adoption. It underscores the need for machine-verifiable controls in benchmark releases to ensure reliability and transparency. Of 300 planned model-prompt calls, 297 yielded nonempty reports, but 60 Claude calls labeled A/B were executed with the same C prompt. Four MONOCHROME1 images lacked required polarity inversion, and an unvalidated extractor truncated five reports to 4000 characters. Reconstructing a cohort of 369 case-finding blocks changed Cochran's Q from 154.73 to 182.29.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Medical-imaging AI benchmarks typically combine datasets, DICOM rendering, prompts, provider APIs, automated labels, statistical code, and manuscripts, with agreement across these artifacts often assumed rather than tested. DICOM is a standard for medical imaging, and polarity inversion is a rendering step necessary for certain monochrome images. Vision-language models (VLMs) are AI systems that process both visual and textual data, and benchmarks like ViLaBench evaluate their capabilities.

## [Sim2Win: Team-Agnostic Football Prediction via Event Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26061) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Sim2Win is a new team-agnostic, event-based system that predicts football match outcomes and profiles tactics without using team identity. It was evaluated on StatsBomb open data from 11 competitions, achieving a mean ROC-AUC of 0.704 and accuracy of 55.4% on unseen teams, outperforming ELO, Pi-Rating, and GAP baselines. This approach addresses a key limitation in football analytics: the inability of identity-based systems to generalize to unseen teams. By relying solely on behavioral tactical features, Sim2Win offers a transferable and practical tool for pre-match decision support, potentially benefiting clubs and analysts who face new opponents regularly. Sim2Win constructs five-match rolling tactical profiles, engineers four interpretable tactical feature ratios, and clusters team behaviors into eight playstyles via K-Means. It trains thirteen classifiers, with CatBoost achieving the strongest in-distribution accuracy of 60.90%, and uses Leave-One-Competition-Out (LOCO) evaluation to test generalization.

Leave-One-Out cross-validation is a rigorous evaluation method that tests a model's ability to generalize to new data, and here it is adapted to competitions to simulate unseen teams.

## [Study: Math Ability Is Single Factor, Not Archetypes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26063) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A study using Bernoulli Mixture Models on 119,034 students' exam results found that mathematical ability is largely a single factor rather than discrete archetypes, challenging common assumptions in personalized learning systems. The best model achieved 78% accuracy, competitive with more complex models while being more explainable. This finding questions the basis of many personalized learning systems that assume discrete, sequential abilities. It suggests that overall ability is the dominant factor, with only small gains from tailoring to specific strengths, potentially influencing future educational AI design. The study analyzed 119,034 students across 13 national-level exams in the UK, classifying question results as pass/fail. The Bernoulli Mixture Model found few distinct clusters, and the high linear correlation between cluster probability distributions supports the single-factor conclusion.

## [FinCacheServe: Dependency-Consistent Answer Reuse for RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26076) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FinCacheServe introduces a dependency-consistent answer reuse mechanism for RAG services over mutable enterprise documents, achieving over 50% reduction in LLM calls without stale outputs. The system is implemented with vLLM and evaluated on financial workloads using Qwen2.5 models. This work addresses a practical and timely problem in LLM serving: reducing GPU-bound generation costs while ensuring correctness in dynamic document environments. It could significantly lower operational costs for enterprises running RAG services over frequently updated documents, making LLM-based analysis more affordable and reliable. On a 2,230-request hosted 7B trace, FinCacheServe skips 53.27% of LLM calls with zero observed dependency-stale outputs. Across three hosted 32B operator-suite seeds, it skips 53.31% of 544 requests, compared with 38.97% for versioned semantic caching and 22.43% for grounded-style reuse.

FinCacheServe treats each generated answer as a serving object indexed by enterprise intent and guarded by document versions, evidence fingerprints, tool fingerprints, model identity, and decoding configuration.

This study introduces a computational framework that integrates computational fluid dynamics (CFD), a genetic algorithm (GA), and a DeepSets neural surrogate to optimize hydrogen leak sensor placement in enclosed infrastructure. The GA achieved a 96.1% detection rate within 60 seconds and reduced blind areas to 0.12%, while the DeepSets surrogate reduced CFD evaluations by 89% and computational time by two orders of magnitude with a fitness gap below 0.01. This work addresses a critical safety issue in emerging hydrogen infrastructure, where current monitoring systems are largely reactive. By enabling proactive sensor placement optimization, the framework can improve detection effectiveness, reduce sensor requirements, and support scalable deployment, potentially impacting safety engineering and smart infrastructure design. The CFD database included 180 scenarios for a 50 m x 30 m x 3 m garage, covering leak rates from 1-150 g/s and ventilation rates of 3-10 ACH. The multi-objective GA showed approximately a 5% improvement in composite fitness over a uniform baseline, and the DeepSets surrogate maintained comparable detection performance and spatial coverage.

## [RAFS: A Reference-Free Score to Detect Silent Reasoning Failures in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26102) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Reasoning Answer Faithfulness Score (RAFS), a reference-free, instance-level diagnostic that evaluates whether an LLM's mathematical chain-of-thought is locally credible, supports its answer, and is stable under resampling and counterfactual interventions. It combines step validity, entailment, counterfactual sensitivity, answer consensus, and conditional reasoning stability. RAFS addresses the reasoning-answer consistency gap, where a correct final answer may hide an invalid derivation, or a valid calculation may be followed by a transcription error. This framework could improve LLM evaluation methodology by providing an auditable warning signal for silent reasoning failures, complementing traditional answer accuracy metrics. The paper formalizes four reasoning-answer outcomes, justifies a non-compensatory aggregator, instantiates semantic trace distance, and quantifies compute and abstention tradeoffs. It also defines verifier independence and power analyses, and includes a preregistered, results-blind confirmatory study on GSM8K and MATH, with a separate feasibility pilot.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) evaluation in LLMs typically focuses on whether the final answer matches a reference, neglecting the correctness of the reasoning process. This can lead to silent reasoning failures where an invalid chain accidentally produces the correct answer. RAFS aims to provide a more faithful evaluation by assessing the reasoning trace itself, without relying on reference answers.

## [TraceCLIP: Training-Free Recovery of Local Semantics from CLIP CLS Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26107) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TraceCLIP is a training-free framework that recovers patch-level semantic evidence by isolating patch-specific terms written into the CLS attention output of CLIP, and converts these into a semantic-geodesic topology gate to calibrate final-layer patch affinity for dense feature reconstruction. On eight zero-shot semantic segmentation benchmarks, it achieves gains of 1.3 to 4.5 points in average mIoU over the strongest prior training-free methods, without additional training or external models. This work addresses a known limitation in CLIP-based dense vision-language understanding, where local semantics are only indirectly constrained by the image-level objective. By providing a training-free method to extract spatially grounded semantic features, it could significantly improve tasks like open-vocabulary segmentation and object localization without the need for additional supervision or task-specific adaptation. The method leverages the observation that patch-specific terms are written into the CLS attention output, and uses a semantic-geodesic topology gate to calibrate final-layer patch affinity. The gains are consistent across both backbones and background settings, and the approach does not require additional training, external vision foundation models, or region-level supervision.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: CLIP is a vision-language model trained on image-text pairs to learn a shared embedding space, but its image-level objective aligns text with a global CLS-derived representation, leaving local vision-language correspondence only indirectly constrained. Dense vision-language tasks like semantic segmentation require associating language concepts with spatially grounded visual regions, which is challenging with standard CLIP features. Existing methods often add supervision or use external models, while training-free approaches typically recover dense responses from patch features without examining where local semantics are most accessible. TraceCLIP builds on the idea that the CLS token aggregates global information, and that patch-level semantics may be recoverable from its internal construction.

## [Framework for Trustworthy Embodied Intelligence with Graded Levels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26121) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a systems framework for trustworthy embodied intelligence, defining 'sustained safe success' as the core objective and proposing four interdependent layers (model, system, evidence, deployment) along with a non-normative hierarchy of trustworthiness levels. This framework addresses a critical gap by emphasizing sustained safe success over mere task completion, which is vital for safety-critical applications like autonomous driving and robotics. It provides a basis for bounded deployment, comparative evaluation, research prioritization, and future standardization in embodied AI. The four layers are: model layer (action proposals with calibrated uncertainty and safety preferences), system layer (dependable execution with safeguards and fault containment), evidence layer (evaluation, verification, and assurance arguments), and deployment layer (runtime monitoring, authority management, and incident response). The trustworthiness levels grade bounded deployment claims across task capability, safety, system assurance, operational governance, and supporting evidence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

This paper draws on fields like robotics, control, dependable computing, and autonomous driving to propose a comprehensive framework.

This paper proposes a stochastic multi-objective bandit framework for selecting k arms to approximate the Pareto frontier, introducing the THV-UCB algorithm with a gap-free regret bound of O~(d√(nkT)). This work addresses an important problem in recommendation systems and multi-objective optimization, providing theoretical guarantees for using small subsets to approximate Pareto fronts, which can improve efficiency and user satisfaction in slate selection tasks. The algorithm selects arms greedily based on optimistic estimates of marginal hypervolume contributions, and the regret is defined with respect to the best size-k subset in hindsight, with an approximation factor α = 1 - 1/e. The paper also provides a gap-dependent bound of O~(nk^{2.5}/Δ_min) that becomes polylogarithmic in T when arms are well separated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-objective bandits extend classic multi-armed bandits to handle multiple reward objectives, where the goal is often to identify the Pareto frontier of non-dominated arms. The hypervolume indicator measures the volume of objective space dominated by a set of points, and greedy maximization for monotone submodular functions provides an approximation guarantee of 1 - 1/e, which is used in this paper to define the regret.

This paper introduces Cognitive Diagnostic Profiling (CDP), a zero-shot framework that prompts LLMs to simulate examinees with diverse cognitive profiles, improving alignment with human response data for psychometric calibration. Using the Tatsuoka fraction-subtraction dataset, CDP improved distributional overlap, mastery-profile correlations (up to 0.98), and item-difficulty recovery across multiple LLM configurations. This work addresses the high cost of human response data in psychometric calibration by making LLM-simulated examinees more realistic, potentially enabling faster and cheaper test development. It bridges AI and educational measurement, offering a practical method for operational test development without extensive human data collection. The study evaluated eight LLM configurations under no-profile, uninformative-CDP, and informative-CDP conditions. In the strongest case (Gemini 3.0 Flash Thinking), 1PL difficulty Spearman correlations rose from 0.24 to 0.86 and 0.90, and RMSE fell from 6.31 to 1.30 and 0.90, with the informative condition most beneficial where profile-level alignment was strong.

## [Pramana: A Composable Backend to Accelerate Empirical Networking Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26352) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Pramana, a composable, domain-specific backend designed to reduce the lag between hypothesis ideation and empirical data generation in networking research. It proposes an intent specification contract that disaggregates experiments into intent, substrate, and mechanism, and demonstrates its utility with a corpus of 255 intents mined from 66 papers, where Pramana's proof-of-concept already satisfies 34% of them. This matters because the ideation-to-data-generation gap is a major bottleneck in networking research, and it will worsen with AI-assisted ideation. Pramana's approach could significantly accelerate empirical validation, benefiting researchers and enabling more efficient AI-assisted research workflows. The intent specification disaggregates an experiment into three independent axes: intent (what data to generate), substrate (where to generate it), and mechanism (how to produce it), allowing one specification to run on any substrate. The paper shows that no existing tool satisfies more than 13% of the mined intents, while Pramana's proof-of-concept achieves 34%, more than twice the best existing tool.

**Background**: Networking research relies on empirical validation, but setting up experiments is often time-consuming and requires starting from scratch. BBR is a congestion control algorithm that uses measurements of delivery rate and round-trip time to model the network path, and it is used as an example in the paper.

This paper introduces a k-order relaxation of the faithfulness assumption to handle higher-order dependencies like XOR and parity relations, and proposes a proof-of-concept algorithm called kOMB for Markov blanket discovery. This work addresses a fundamental limitation in Markov blanket discovery, potentially improving causal discovery and feature selection in scenarios where faithfulness is violated. It could lead to more robust methods for learning graphical models from data. The kOMB algorithm uses the k-order relaxation to capture parity-type relationships between k+2 variables. The paper provides empirical evidence that kOMB can recover the Markov blanket under both true and empirical violations of faithfulness, with code available on GitHub.

**Background**: The Markov blanket of a variable is the minimal set of variables that makes it conditionally independent of all others, and it is crucial for feature selection and causal discovery. This paper relaxes that assumption to handle such cases.

Voice Memory introduces an inference-only, memory-coupled listener-thinker architecture for agentic speech recognition, reducing over-correction errors from 64% to 35% on financial news and improving weighted word error rate from 8.36% to 7.52% across ten HyPoradise domains. This work addresses a practical limitation of generative error correction in ASR, where over-correction can degrade accuracy. By providing a portable, auditable, and zero-parameter solution, it could enhance the reliability of agentic speech recognition systems across various domains. The architecture uses a frozen corrector that reads a per-domain memory.md file and decides whether to act on the hypothesis or abstain, while a score-gated optimizer asynchronously revises the memory only when edits strictly improve a held-out score. The method adds zero parameters to the inference path and transfers across corrector families.

## [FAS-R1: Unified Multi-Task MLLM for Reasoning Face Anti-Spoofing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26432) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FAS-R1 introduces a two-stage reasoning-oriented MLLM framework for face anti-spoofing, combining cold-start supervised fine-tuning on a new long-CoT dataset (FAS-R1-23K) with GRPO-based post-training. It achieves 98.75% authenticity accuracy, 93.33% attack-type accuracy, and 96.30/94.73% AP@40/AP@50 in-domain, outperforming existing methods in cross-domain generalization. This work advances face anti-spoofing by moving beyond simple binary classification to provide attack semantics and image-grounded evidence, which is crucial for human inspection and security applications. The integration of GRPO and difficulty-aware training addresses the under-optimization of difficult attacks, potentially improving robustness in real-world scenarios. The framework uses Degradation-Simulated Augmentation (DSA) to encourage stable spoof-cue reasoning across visual-quality shifts, and Difficulty-Aware GRPO (DA-GRPO) to mitigate easy-sample dominance. The main model is a 3B parameter MLLM, and experiments show favorable scaling behavior with different base models; code will be released soon.

This paper introduces a model-side aggregation interface that uses HyperLogLog (HLL) sketches to maintain compact, mergeable aggregation states for long-context language models. The approach achieves high accuracy on set-based tasks while keeping a fixed 2 KiB state size, independent of context length or cardinality. Long-context language models often struggle with non-additive, set-based aggregation tasks as context grows, limiting their reliability in real-world applications like log analysis and data processing. Merge tests with up to 256 segments produced identical readouts to a single pass, and on 3,969 aggregate-then-reason tasks, the method reached 99.2% accuracy on Gemma 4 (31B, BF16) compared to 100.0% with exact aggregation.

## [ForgetBench: New Benchmark for Measuring Forgetting in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26455) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ForgetBench is a new benchmark that systematically characterizes forgetting behavior in large language models under continual knowledge editing. It introduces concept-based and scenario-based QA paradigms to disentangle isolated factual retention from structured relational knowledge preservation. This benchmark addresses the underexplored problem of long-term knowledge retention in LLMs, which is critical for real-world applications where models are continuously updated. The findings highlight the need for more robust memory mechanisms, potentially influencing future model design and evaluation practices. ForgetBench builds a sequential editing framework with temporally ordered knowledge streams and evaluates models across multiple editing stages. It introduces a unified evaluation framework that models knowledge evolution over time, measuring temporal decay, retention strength, and cross-instance stability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) store knowledge in their parameters (parametric memory) during training, but this knowledge can become outdated or be overwritten when models are updated through continual learning or knowledge editing. Existing evaluation methods often focus on single-step edits or static knowledge, failing to capture the temporal dynamics of retention and degradation. ForgetBench aims to fill this gap by providing a systematic way to measure forgetting over time.

## [LLMET Framework Evaluates M3D Memories for Energy-Efficient LLM Serving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26491) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LLMET, a validated cross-layer simulation framework, is introduced to evaluate the impact of monolithic 3D (M3D) on-chip memories on the energy efficiency of LLM serving. Simulations show that expanding L2 cache from 40MB to 1GB reduces chip energy by 44% during Llama3.1-70B prefill on dual A100 GPUs. This work addresses the critical challenge of energy consumption in LLM serving, which is becoming a major bottleneck as deployments scale. By demonstrating the potential of M3D memories to reduce off-chip data movement, it provides a path toward more energy-efficient hardware-software co-design for AI systems. The framework is validated and applied across multiple platforms, including dual NVIDIA A100 and 8x NVIDIA B200-like systems. On the B200-like platform, increasing L2 cache from 128MB to 4GB saves up to 24% prefill energy, while edge workloads see 30% decode energy savings when cache grows from 8MB to 256MB.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Monolithic 3D (M3D) integration is a semiconductor manufacturing technique that stacks device layers vertically, enabling denser and larger on-chip memories. Back-End-Of-Line (BEOL) refers to the final stage of chip fabrication where metal interconnect layers are deposited, and M3D can integrate memory at this level. LLM serving relies heavily on data movement between on-chip cache and off-chip HBM, which is a major source of energy consumption.

## [CWAC: Collaborative Weighting with Pessimistic Critic to Reduce Overestimation Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26509) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Collaborative Weighting Actor-Critic (CWAC), a framework that combines distributional critics with a collaborative weighting mechanism to reweight TD-errors and uncertainty, along with a stochastic pessimistic value estimation scheme. It can be integrated into existing off-policy algorithms like SAC, TD3, and DDPG, and shows significant performance improvements across various simulated tasks. Overestimation bias is a well-known problem in off-policy reinforcement learning that degrades training stability and performance. CWAC offers a unified solution that explicitly handles predictive uncertainty, potentially improving the reliability and efficiency of continuous control agents, which is crucial for real-world applications. CWAC employs distributional critics to model return uncertainty and introduces a collaborative weighting mechanism that jointly reweights TD-errors and uncertainty, enabling robust learning from reliable samples while suppressing noisy updates. It also incorporates a stochastic pessimistic value estimation scheme via sampling from the return distribution, which mitigates error propagation during policy improvement. The code is publicly available at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/CWAC-348E.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: In off-policy reinforcement learning, temporal-difference (TD) learning introduces noisy targets, and greedy policy updates amplify early-stage estimation errors, leading to persistent overestimation bias. Existing methods like prioritized sampling or modified value learning objectives often overemphasize high-uncertainty transitions, further amplifying bias. Distributional reinforcement learning, which predicts the entire distribution of future rewards rather than just the expected return, has shown benefits in improving agent performance.

## [Shared Symbolic Backbones for Multi-Output Symbolic Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26528) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a neuro-evolutionary symbolic regression method that discovers a shared symbolic backbone for coupled multi-output systems, allowing latent symbolic units to be reused across outputs via sparse additive or multiplicative read-outs. The method evolves discrete structures with mutation and crossover while tuning continuous parameters via gradient descent, and it is validated on benchmarks and a hydrothermal liquefaction case. This work addresses a key limitation of standard symbolic regression, which typically treats outputs independently, leading to inconsistent models for coupled physical systems. By enforcing cross-output consistency through a shared backbone, the method improves interpretability and can aid in diagnosing weakly identifiable shared mechanisms, potentially benefiting fields like process systems engineering. The method is not a general-purpose predictor; its main value lies in extracting sparse, shared, weakly identifiable mechanisms, as shown in Langmuir-Hinshelwood and site-coverage denominators where independent PySR fails to close the consistency gap. In contrast, for identifiable outputs like the Van de Vusse benchmark, independent regression matches or outperforms the coupled model.

Neuro-evolution combines evolutionary algorithms with neural network concepts to optimize both structure and parameters. In multi-output systems, outputs are often coupled through shared physical parameters, but traditional SR treats each output independently, potentially yielding inconsistent equations.

AgentGFM introduces a graph foundation model where each node acts as an agent to autonomously control information propagation through a predict-act-observe-correct process. This paper introduces a novel persona-based index that uses generative AI to predict FOMC rate decisions, constructing nearly 25,000 retrievable chunks from public data and partitioning them into per-member corpora. The index tracks the rate cycle from 2022 to 2025 with a Kendall's tau of 0.68 and leads the federal funds target rate by roughly three quarters. This work demonstrates the first ability to capture time-varying group behavior via a collection of digital personas, offering a new tool for economic forecasting and central bank policy prediction. It could enhance market participants' and policymakers' understanding of FOMC decisions, potentially improving investment strategies and policy anticipation. The personas are evaluated on identifiability and detectability, with average member-conditional recall 8 times chance and generated content nearly indistinguishable from real content (tau_det = 0.23 vs 0.15 floor). Query-conditioned representations outperform retrieval-only ones in capturing hawk-dove stance (Kendall's tau = 0.63, p < 0.001) and enable a simple classifier with 0.69 accuracy versus 0.47 base rate.

ServerlessT2I is a new serverless-native system that decomposes text-to-image (T2I) workflows into independently managed model functions, enabling per-model scaling, declarative composition, and fairness-aware scheduling. It harvests slack GPU memory to reduce overheads and sustains up to 2x higher request rates or saves up to 3x GPU resources under the same SLOs. By enabling fine-grained decomposition and fairness, it could significantly improve resource utilization and multi-tenant performance for T2I workloads, benefiting cloud providers and users alike. ServerlessT2I uses a data plane built from slack GPU memory to reduce model loading and data communication overheads. It also introduces a fair scheduler for multi-tenant serving, validated with production traces showing up to 2x higher request rates or 3x GPU savings while meeting SLOs.

Existing platforms typically treat each workflow as an opaque GPU function, provisioning and scaling all models together, which obscures structure and inflates overhead. ServerlessT2I proposes decomposing workflows into independent model functions to enable finer-grained management and scheduling.

## [Physics-Informed LLM Framework for PID Tuning in Chemical Processes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26594) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel framework that uses large and small language models (LLMs/SLMs) to automate PID tuning for chemical processes, mimicking the iterative workflow of plant engineers. It also proposes PI-GRPO, a physics-informed reinforcement learning algorithm, to fine-tune a local small model (Qwen3-0.6B) for improved tuning performance. PID tuning is a critical and widespread task in process control, and automating it with AI can significantly reduce manual effort and improve efficiency in industrial settings. This framework demonstrates that both hosted LLMs and locally deployable small models can achieve high success rates, making advanced AI-based control accessible even in resource-constrained environments. The framework uses IMC-based demonstrations and closed-loop response features to guide LLMs in generating and correcting PID gains. On 100 FOPDT and 100 SOPDT test cases, hosted LLMs (DeepSeek-V4-Flash and Qwen3.7-Plus) achieved final success rates of 75-89% and 77-79%, respectively, while the fine-tuned Qwen3-0.6B reached 94.0% success after PI-GRPO, up from 86.5% after SFT.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: PID controllers are widely used in industrial process control, and tuning their parameters is essential for optimal performance. Traditional tuning methods often rely on process models, but engineers frequently adjust parameters iteratively based on observed responses. Internal Model Control (IMC) is a model-based tuning approach that simplifies PID design. Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) is a reinforcement learning algorithm that fine-tunes LLMs by comparing groups of outputs, and PI-GRPO extends it with physics-informed rewards.

<a id="item-189"></a>
## [Decoupled Visual Processing: Efficient Multimodal LLM Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26596) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Decoupled Visual Processing (DVP), a training framework that replaces the upper decoder layers of a pretrained LLM with a single lightweight transformer block dedicated to visual tokens, reducing trainable parameters during visual instruction tuning. Experiments on LLaVA-1.5 show competitive performance on MME, POPE, and ChartQA benchmarks while training only a fraction of the parameters. This work addresses a significant computational bottleneck in fine-tuning multimodal LLMs, offering a parameter-efficient alternative that could lower the barrier for adapting large models to vision-language tasks. It also provides insights into the divergent representational needs of visual and textual tokens in deep transformer layers, potentially influencing future architecture designs. DVP splits visual and textual tokens after the first half of the decoder layers: visual tokens pass through a newly initialized single transformer block, while textual tokens continue through the original frozen decoder layers, then the streams are concatenated before the language modeling head. Only the single transformer block is updated during training, dramatically reducing trainable parameters.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) integrate visual and textual understanding within a unified transformer architecture, but fine-tuning all parameters for visual instruction tuning is computationally expensive. Visual instruction tuning adapts LLMs to follow instructions involving images, and LLaVA is a pioneering framework in this area. DVP exploits the observation that visual and textual tokens have diverging representation requirements in deeper layers, enabling a decoupled, parameter-efficient pathway.

## [WhisperRec: Latent Reasoning for Efficient Foundation Recommendation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26621) ⭐️ 7.0/10

WhisperRec introduces a latent reasoning framework that compresses teacher-generated Chain-of-Thought (CoT) into learnable latent tokens, enabling a Latent-Reason-then-Answer paradigm for foundation recommendation models (FRMs). It outperforms explicit-CoT methods, improving SID@64 by 17.44% and 9.33% over Think and No-Think variants, respectively, and achieves over 10x higher online inference throughput. This work addresses a critical efficiency bottleneck in LLM-based recommendation systems by eliminating verbose rationale generation, which could enable real-time, large-scale deployment of reasoning-capable recommenders. It also advances the trend of compressing Chain-of-Thought into latent space, potentially influencing broader LLM reasoning applications. WhisperRec uses Multi-View Adaptive CoT (MV-ACoT) to generate diverse, instance-adaptive supervision, and a three-stage Latent Reasoning Alignment procedure to internalize teacher CoT into latent representations. Experiments were conducted on an industrial-scale Kuaishou dataset and the public Kuaishou LLM-Rec benchmark, demonstrating consistent improvements over baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Foundation recommendation models (FRMs) leverage large language models (LLMs) for their reasoning capabilities, often using explicit Chain-of-Thought (CoT) under a Think-then-Answer paradigm. However, generating lengthy rationales incurs high inference latency and fixed CoT templates fail to capture diverse user interests. WhisperRec addresses this by compressing CoT into latent tokens, a technique related to recent research on CoT compression, which aims to reduce token generation costs while preserving reasoning quality.

## [Context Sampling in TabPFN: Larger Contexts Improve Stability and Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26628) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper empirically studies how context size and sampling methods affect prediction stability and accuracy in TabPFN on small tabular datasets, finding that larger contexts improve both. It also shows that random sampling is as accurate as expensive methods like K-Means and farthest-point sampling, while being much cheaper. These findings provide practical guidance for practitioners using TabPFN on small datasets, helping them choose context sizes and sampling methods without unnecessary computational cost. The results also deepen understanding of in-context learning mechanisms, highlighting the role of feature-space coverage over distribution matching. The study used 15 OpenML datasets and found that AUC coefficient of variation decreased from 6-18% at k=16 to 1-4% at larger context sizes. Mixed-effects analysis showed diversity and coverage are stronger predictors of accuracy than feature-mean matching (diversity beta=+0.23, p=3x10^-12; feature-mean shift beta=-0.01, p=0.71).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: TabPFN is a transformer-based model for tabular data that performs classification via in-context learning, conditioning on labeled training rows without gradient updates. In-context learning is a paradigm where models adapt to new tasks by conditioning on examples in the prompt, without parameter optimization. Farthest-point sampling is a technique to select diverse points, often used in 3D object detection and clustering.

## [MalGuard: Graph-Based Malware Detection with Operational Roles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26634) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MalGuard, a novel graph-based malware detection method that identifies cohesive groups of basic blocks as operational roles and learns expressive program graph representations by modeling interactions among these roles, preserving sparse malicious signals, and capturing hierarchical graph structure. Extensive experiments show that MalGuard improves detection performance and reduces the expected cost of undetected malware. MalGuard addresses limitations of byte-based ML and existing graph methods, offering a more robust approach against evasive malware that manipulates raw bytes. This is significant for organizational cybersecurity, as it can improve malware detection accuracy and reduce financial and operational risks associated with undetected threats. The method introduces two innovations: an operational role identification approach and a program graph representation learning method. The former identifies cohesive basic block groups that jointly realize meaningful program behaviors, while the latter models interactions among operational roles, preserves sparse malicious signals, and captures hierarchical graph structure.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Malware detection is a critical cybersecurity task. Traditional byte-based machine learning methods are vulnerable to evasion techniques that manipulate raw bytes. Graph-based methods represent software as program graphs (e.g., control flow graphs) to capture execution behavior, but they often fail to explicitly identify cohesive groups of basic blocks or learn sufficiently expressive representations. Basic blocks are straight-line code sequences with a single entry and exit, serving as atomic nodes in control flow graphs.

## [FakeIDet3-DB: First Comprehensive Database of Digital Manipulations on Real IDs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26641) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FakeIDet3-DB introduces the first comprehensive database of digital manipulations on real government-issued IDs, including classical and GAN-based attacks, with GDPR-compliant patch extraction. The proposed PACE algorithm extracts nearly 5.2M patches from over 6.4K images, and evaluation shows state-of-the-art models struggle with detection (32.45% EER) and localization (83.48% AUC-ROC). This database addresses a critical gap in training forensic models on real vs. synthetic data, improving the robustness of ID authentication systems against advanced generative attacks. It is timely for security and AI research, especially given the rise of GAN-based manipulations and strict privacy regulations like GDPR. The database includes classical manipulations like copy-move and generative techniques such as face-swapping and inpainting, enhanced with artifact suppression. PACE uses Integral Image mapping and distance-driven Non-Maximum Suppression to maximize semantic density while preventing PII leakage, yielding 5.2M patches from 6.4K+ images.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Identity document authentication relies on complex security patterns, but generative AI can now create high-fidelity manipulations that bypass verification. Training forensic models is hindered by privacy regulations, so synthetic templates are often used, but they lack real-world complexity. This database bridges that gap by providing real ID manipulations with GDPR-compliant patch extraction.

## [FPSGen: BEV-Supported Transport Flows for Flexible Point Cloud Scene Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26645) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FPSGen introduces a flexible point cloud scene generation framework that constructs point sources independently of partial LiDAR scans, using a BEV prior and a teacher-student approximate optimal transport scheme to achieve state-of-the-art performance on SemanticKITTI completion and KITTI-360 unconditional generation. This work addresses a critical train-inference mismatch in LiDAR-conditioned point cloud generation, enabling more robust and flexible scene generation for autonomous driving and 3D vision. It also provides a unified framework for both unconditional and cue-conditioned generation, potentially simplifying deployment in real-world applications. FPSGen predicts a BEV prior with density, height, and mask channels, then samples the density map to form a BEV-supported point source. The teacher-student approximate optimal transport scheme learns a velocity field that induces straighter transport paths, achieving strong performance even with a single point transport step.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Point cloud generation for outdoor scenes often relies on LiDAR-conditioned completion, where noisy point clouds are created by perturbing ground-truth scenes during training but initialized from partial scans during inference, causing sparsity and visibility bias. BEV (bird's-eye view) representations are commonly used in autonomous driving to provide a top-down view of the scene. Transport flows, such as optimal transport and flow matching, are generative models that learn to transform noise into data distributions.

The approach pre-generates adversarial examples and dynamically displays them to compromise the victim model without any modification. By demonstrating a stealthy physical attack, it highlights vulnerabilities in current deep learning systems and underscores the need for robust testing and defense mechanisms. The method generates a large number of adversarial examples in advance and computes them in real time to dynamically display infrared patterns. Experiments show the attack is effective across diverse lighting conditions, varying object motion velocities, and different object placements, impairing the network's optical flow estimation accuracy.

The survey identified 34 papers from 1,589 screened records using a Boolean search over the OpenAIRE Graph, a comprehensive open knowledge graph of research outputs.
