## [ERAHBO: Efficient Risk-Averse Bayesian Optimization for AutoRL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26680) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ERAHBO, a novel Bayesian optimization method that models both the mean and variance of RL outcomes as functions of hyperparameters, using adaptive re-sampling to improve sample efficiency. It aims to find hyperparameter configurations that achieve high average return while reducing variability across training runs. This work addresses the important problem of risk-aware hyperparameter tuning in reinforcement learning, where outcomes are highly stochastic. By improving sample efficiency and reducing variability, ERAHBO could make AutoRL more practical for real-world applications, benefiting both the RL and AutoML communities. ERAHBO uses a heteroscedastic Gaussian process to model input-dependent noise, and employs an adaptive re-sampling strategy that allocates evaluations based on estimated uncertainty. Empirical evaluations across diverse RL algorithms and environments show that ERAHBO generally outperforms both risk-neutral and risk-averse baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Bayesian optimization is a sequential design strategy for global optimization of black-box functions, often used for hyperparameter tuning. In reinforcement learning, hyperparameters significantly affect both performance and variability, and standard BO typically optimizes only the expected value, ignoring risk. Heteroscedastic Bayesian optimization extends BO to handle input-dependent noise, enabling risk-averse optimization by trading off mean and variance.

## [MPEcho Improves Cover Song Generation with Phoneme-Level Conditioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26698) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MPEcho, a new framework built on SongEcho, integrates a phoneme encoder and a length regulator to provide explicit phoneme-level conditioning and precise temporal boundaries, significantly reducing phoneme error rate (PER) in cover song generation. It also introduces Phonsa, a Whisper-based automatic transcription model for high-precision phoneme annotations of singing voices. This work addresses a known limitation of SongEcho—lyric accuracy—by incorporating explicit phoneme conditioning, which could improve the intelligibility of generated vocals in cover songs and related applications like singing voice synthesis. The introduction of Phonsa also provides a practical tool for phoneme-level annotation, potentially benefiting the broader music and speech research community. MPEcho is built upon SongEcho, which itself uses F0 sequences and voiced/unvoiced (V/UV) tags for conditioning, but these implicit cues fail to guarantee lyric accuracy. The proposed Phonsa model overcomes the scarcity of high-quality audio-phoneme pairs by providing precise phoneme-level annotations, and experimental results validate its effectiveness for alignment and MPEcho for end-to-end cover song generation.

Phoneme error rate (PER) is a metric used to evaluate the accuracy of phoneme-level transcription, commonly used in speech recognition and synthesis. Whisper is an open-source speech recognition model by OpenAI that can be fine-tuned for phoneme transcription tasks.

## [FARI: One-Step Robust Inversion for Diffusion Watermarking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26723) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces FARI, a one-step inversion framework for watermarking in diffusion models, which leverages the low curvature of inversion trajectories to achieve fast and robust watermark verification. It also incorporates lightweight adversarial LoRA fine-tuning of the denoiser, requiring only about 20 minutes of training on a single NVIDIA RTX A6000 GPU. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in inversion-based watermarking, which is often slow and error-prone, by enabling faster and more robust verification. It could significantly impact AI-generated content authentication, making it more practical for real-world deployment. FARI surpasses 50-step DDIM inversion in watermark-verification robustness while dramatically reducing inference time. The method is based on two key observations: inversion trajectories have lower curvature than forward generation paths, and in watermark verification, external distortions dominate the error, making the speed-truncation trade-off less critical.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models generate images by iteratively denoising a Gaussian noise vector through a series of steps, and inversion-based watermarking embeds a watermark into the generated image and later inverts the generation process to extract it. However, traditional inversion methods require many function evaluations (NFEs), making them slow and sensitive to truncation errors, which hampers their practical use. FARI addresses this by exploiting the low curvature of inversion trajectories to enable one-step inversion, combined with adversarial fine-tuning to enhance robustness against distortions.

The paper introduces Dualin, a two-stage method that jointly recovers the semantic prompt and latent noise of a target image in text-to-image diffusion models. It integrates CLIP and a large language model for prompt inversion, followed by unconditional DDIM inversion to reconstruct the exact latent noise. This addresses a key limitation in prompt inversion by recognizing that latent noise, which encodes structural information, is also crucial for visual fidelity. Extensive experiments on diverse datasets show that Dualin generates high-quality inverted prompts and achieves state-of-the-art image fidelity.

Diffusion models generate images by iteratively denoising random noise, and the latent noise carries structural information about the image.

This study introduces a multimodal deep learning framework that integrates CNN-based image analysis with osteometric measurements for avian bone classification, achieving 86% accuracy in skeletal element identification and 75% top-3 accuracy in family-level taxonomic classification. This work demonstrates the feasibility of combining visual and morphometric data for automated zooarchaeological identification, potentially streamlining archaeological analysis and reducing reliance on expert manual examination. It also establishes a methodological baseline for future AI-assisted tools in this niche but important field. The framework uses a two-stage segmentation pipeline combining BiRefNet and SAM2, and a pre-trained EfficientNet_V2_S backbone for visual feature extraction, fused with standardized morphometric data via feature-level fusion. The dataset comprises over 10,000 images from multiple museum and research collections.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Zooarchaeology involves identifying animal remains from archaeological sites, which is often time-consuming and requires specialized expertise. Deep learning, particularly convolutional neural networks (CNNs), has been applied to image classification tasks, but its use in zooarchaeology is limited. BiRefNet is a bilateral reference network for high-resolution dichotomous image segmentation, while SAM2 (Segment Anything Model 2) is a foundation model by Meta for promptable segmentation. EfficientNetV2 is a family of efficient convolutional networks known for faster training and better parameter efficiency.

## [Recurrent Decoders Outperform Mamba in Intracortical Brain-to-Text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26751) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This study evaluates a controlled 2x2 grid of decoder architectures (GRU vs. hybrid Mamba) and output targets (phonetic vs. character) for intracortical brain-to-text on the Brain-to-Text '25 benchmark. The recurrent baseline (GRU) remains strongest, achieving 12.62% PER and 21.19% WER with phonetic targets, while the Mamba hybrid is competitive but does not surpass it. This work provides valuable empirical insights into underexplored design axes for brain-to-text systems, informing future architecture choices for BCI and NLP researchers. It suggests that recurrent decoders remain a strong baseline, potentially guiding resource allocation in neuroprosthesis development. The study uses a CTC objective under a reproducible protocol, with ablations isolating architectural contributions and error analysis showing representation-dependent failures: articulatory-like phoneme confusions vs. lexical and word-boundary errors. The best textual GRU after LM rescoring reaches 13.39% CER and 26.28% WER.

**Background**: Intracortical brain-to-text systems decode neural activity into text, often pairing a neural-sequence phone decoder with an external language model. Selective state-space models (SSMs), such as Mamba, are a recent architecture class offering linear-time sequence modeling, potentially more efficient than Transformers. CTC (Connectionist Temporal Classification) is a sequence-level objective enabling end-to-end training without frame-wise alignments, commonly used in speech recognition and neural decoding.

## [Data Augmentation Search Boosts OOD Skin Cancer Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26765) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This study systematically searched data augmentation strategies for dermoscopic skin cancer classification, finding that photometric transformations and a mix policy significantly improve out-of-domain (OOD) generalization. On an expanded held-out pool, the mix policy improved ROC-AUC by +0.053 (95% CI +0.045 to +0.061, p<0.001), consistent across four training seeds. This work addresses a critical challenge in medical imaging: models often fail when deployed on data from different sources or devices. By identifying augmentation strategies that model real domain shifts, it provides practical guidance for building more robust skin cancer classifiers, potentially improving diagnostic reliability in diverse clinical settings. The study used a ConvNeXt-Large backbone and ROC-AUC metric, with splits at the lesion-ID level. HAM10000 and ISIC 2019-2020 were held out as source-disjoint OOD test sets. On a small independent clinical collection, single-checkpoint sensitivity rose from 0.591 to 0.818, but this was based on only 22 malignant cases and did not persist across seeds.

**Background**: Data augmentation is a technique that artificially expands training data by applying transformations like rotation, scaling, or color adjustments, helping models generalize better. Out-of-domain (OOD) generalization refers to a model's ability to perform well on data from a different distribution than the training data, which is crucial in medical imaging where devices and conditions vary. Dermoscopic skin cancer classification uses deep learning to distinguish malignant from benign lesions, but accuracy often drops under domain shift.

## [MediaWiki Code2Code Search: Neural Retrieval for Semantic Code Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26766) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MediaWiki Code2Code Search introduces a neural retrieval system that indexes 1.29 million structural entities across 2,500+ MediaWiki repositories, enabling semantic code-to-code discovery. It uses a split-build architecture with a FAISS IVF-PQ index, achieving a 96.6% memory reduction and a median query latency of 1.85 seconds on commodity hardware. This system addresses the lexical gap in code search by retrieving based on computational intent rather than surface tokens, improving precision over traditional BM25. It demonstrates a practical deployment of neural retrieval at scale within the constraints of Wikimedia Toolforge, offering an open RESTful API that could benefit the broader software engineering community. The FAISS IVF-PQ index occupies 168.6 MB, a 96.6% reduction compared to a flat float32 baseline, and fits within the 6 GiB RAM limit of Wikimedia Toolforge. Evaluation on a 27-query benchmark shows a P@10 of 0.87 (strict matching 0.52) versus BM25's 0.64 (0.34), with gains most pronounced in name-obfuscated tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Code search in large-scale ecosystems often struggles with the lexical gap between user queries and implementation details. Traditional information retrieval (IR) methods like BM25 rely on lexical matching, while deep learning (DL) approaches offer better semantic understanding but often require significant computational resources. FAISS IVF-PQ is an approximate nearest neighbor index that compresses vectors for memory efficiency while maintaining search speed. Wikimedia Toolforge is a free cloud hosting platform for tools that support the Wikimedia movement, with limited resources.

## [Journey Operators Framework for Multi-Axis Data Composition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26775) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a novel framework called Journey Operators for modeling multi-axis data structures, where composition and movement are path-independent across axes. It proves that under certain symmetry assumptions, the pairwise scoring rule must take the form of block-wise rotations, and proposes the JoFormer model for value aggregation. This framework provides a theoretical foundation for understanding and designing positional encodings like RoPE, potentially leading to more efficient and interpretable models in AI/ML. It could impact various domains including vision, language, and length generalization, offering content-adaptive inductive biases. The framework recovers Rotary Position Embedding (RoPE) and its multi-dimensional variants when transformations are fixed. It also proves that path-independence holds exactly when axis transformations commute, and under toral-frame symmetry, cocycle, bilinearity, and norm-preservation assumptions, the scoring rule must be block-wise rotations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Many types of data have structure along multiple axes, such as words in a sentence or pixels in an image. Along a single axis, order matters, but across independent axes, composition should be order-independent. This work formalizes such multi-axis structure using journey operators, which are products of per-axis transformations along a path, and connects to existing positional encoding methods like RoPE.

## [KQSP: Training-Free Method Enforces Crossing-Free Probabilistic K-Line Forecasts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26792) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces KQSP (K-line–Quantile Sequential Projection), a parameter-free and training-free reconciliation method that enforces both quantile and K-line crossing-free properties in probabilistic OHLC forecasts. It is applicable to any forecasting model and reduces crossing rates to zero on all tested data. This method addresses two consistency issues in probabilistic K-line forecasting without requiring model retraining or specialized architectures, making it a practical and model-agnostic solution. It could improve the reliability of financial forecasts and be adopted across various forecasting models, potentially benefiting traders and risk analysts. KQSP is a sequential projection method that corrects forecasts to satisfy both quantile and K-line ordering constraints. It preserves predictive accuracy while producing substantially smaller corrections compared to existing methods, and it has been validated using various models, including pretrained foundation models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Probabilistic K-line forecasting predicts uncertainty in open, high, low, and close (OHLC) prices. Two common issues are quantile crossing (where a higher quantile forecast falls below a lower one) and K-line crossing (where the forecast low exceeds the open/close or the high falls below them). Existing solutions typically address only one issue through output reordering, specialized architectures, or penalized training objectives.

## [AI as Friction for Reflection in Design Ideation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26827) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper argues that generative AI tools for design ideation should introduce friction to support reflection-in-action, rather than merely smoothing the creative process. It proposes reframing AI as a 'friction agent for reflection' instead of a 'smoothing agent for output.' This perspective challenges the dominant assumption in HCI and AI-assisted creativity that frictionless experiences are always better. It could influence the design of future AI tools to better support designers' rationale-building and collaborative ideation, impacting both research and practice. The paper emphasizes the importance of reflection-in-action, where accepting, rejecting, and reworking ideas helps designers develop rationale. It highlights that this is especially critical in group ideation, where ideas must be expressed and explained to others for further extension or rejection.

**Background**: Reflection-in-action is a concept from Donald Schön's work, referring to thinking while doing, as opposed to reflection-on-action, which happens after the fact. In HCI, 'friction' is often seen as an obstacle, but recent research explores 'positive friction' that can promote reflection and better outcomes. Generative AI tools in design typically aim to reduce friction for faster output, but this paper suggests that may undermine deeper cognitive processes.

## [OPAB: A New Method to Detect Outcome Performativity via Interventions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26908) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces OPAB (Outcome Performativity A/B Detection), an intervention-based method to detect outcome performativity by comparing outcome distributions across different prediction groups. It provides sample complexity bounds and empirical validation, including a case study on the Open Bandits dataset. Outcome performativity is a critical issue in domains like recommender systems and credit assignment, where predictions can influence the outcomes they predict. OPAB offers a formal, theoretically grounded approach to detect this phenomenon, which could improve the reliability and fairness of ML systems in practice. OPAB detects outcome performativity by assessing the dissimilarity in outcome distributions produced by different prediction groups (interventions). The paper derives sample complexity bounds under various outcome performative assumption classes and identifies regions of indistinguishability where detection is infeasible with limited interventions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Outcome performativity refers to situations where predictions causally influence the outcomes they predict, such as in palliative care, credit assignment, and recommender systems. Causal inference methods are often used to estimate intervention effects, but detecting performativity requires comparing outcomes under different interventions. OPAB leverages this idea by formalizing an A/B testing approach to detect significant differences in outcome distributions.

## [BioVLN: A Simulation Platform for Biomedical Lab Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26914) ⭐️ 7.0/10

BioVLN is a new simulation platform for visual-language navigation in biomedical laboratories, representing each instrument with physical, clearance, and operation regions. It provides 47 scenes and 1667 episodes, and experiments show that sampling multiple valid positions in the operation area improves success to 83.3–92.5% while reducing unsafe proximity. This platform addresses a critical gap in embodied navigation, as existing platforms are designed for household environments and fail to model the functional requirements of laboratory instruments. By enabling safe and functional navigation in biomedical labs, BioVLN could accelerate research in embodied AI and robotics for scientific settings, potentially improving automation in laboratories. BioVLN models each instrument with three regions: physical body, clearance region, and operation area, applied consistently to scene generation, target placement, navigation evaluation, and safety analysis. It supports procedural scene generation and manually designed environments, and provides standardized navigation and reinforcement-learning interfaces for trajectory collection and policy training.

**Background**: Visual-language navigation (VLN) is a task where an embodied agent follows natural language instructions to navigate in a 3D environment. Existing VLN platforms, such as AI2-THOR, focus on household scenes and treat targets as object centers, which is inadequate for laboratory instruments that require approach from a specific operating side. BioVLN addresses this by introducing a region-based representation that captures the functional aspects of instruments, enabling more realistic and safe navigation in biomedical labs.

## [FedDAB: Two-Phase Defense Against Backdoor Attacks in Federated Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26933) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes FedDAB, a two-phase defense method that combines local contrastive regularization with alignment checking to defend against backdoor attacks in federated learning. It introduces a model-contrastive term in the local objective and an alignment checking strategy to filter malicious updates, with a theoretical convergence rate of O(1/T). Federated learning is vulnerable to backdoor attacks, and existing defenses often fail due to statistical heterogeneity and stealthy attacks. FedDAB addresses these limitations, potentially improving security in edge computing and distributed AI systems, benefiting the AI/ML security community. FedDAB operates in two phases: first, it adds a model-contrastive term to the local objective to enhance consistency among benign updates; second, it uses alignment checking to evaluate updates based on overall-direction and parameter-level alignment with historical information, excluding abnormal ones. The method is theoretically proven to converge at O(1/T) and empirically outperforms existing defenses.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Federated learning (FL) is a distributed machine learning paradigm where multiple clients train a shared model without sharing raw data. However, its distributed nature makes it susceptible to backdoor attacks, where malicious clients inject poisoned updates to cause the global model to misbehave on specific triggers. Existing defenses often overlook the natural deviations among benign updates due to non-IID data and the stealthiness of backdoor attacks, limiting their effectiveness. Model-contrastive learning, as in MOON, adapts contrastive learning principles to the model level to improve representation consistency.

## [SymmGrid Accelerates On-Robot Learning with Parallelized Symmetries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26985) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SymmGrid, a trajectory-level augmentation framework, super-scales group transformations to accelerate on-robot reinforcement learning in both egocentric and exocentric visual setups. It achieves wall-clock training convergence speed-ups of 1.37-2.17x and evaluation success rate improvements of 1.09x-1.27x on real robot manipulation tasks. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in on-robot learning: slow wall-clock training times. By significantly speeding up training and improving performance, SymmGrid brings us closer to sub-10 minute on-robot learning, which could accelerate deployment of robotic manipulation in real-world applications. SymmGrid models an MDP under a symmetry tree, using homographies to warp visual scenes for spatial transformations. It was evaluated on real robot tasks including peg-insertions, cable routing, and object relocations, achieving fastest training convergence times of 16.6, 10.9, and 79.3 minutes respectively, and nAUC improvements up to 2.59x.

Egocentric vision captures the scene from the robot's perspective, while exocentric vision provides an external view, both of which are important for robot perception.

## [SciFigAlign: Fine-tuned Multimodal Scorer for Scientific Figures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27066) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SciFigAlign, a fine-tuned multimodal scorer that evaluates scientific figures on Clarity, Relevance, Informativeness, and Structure, using a new dataset of 3,857 figures from peer-reviewed conference papers. It achieves a macro MAE of 0.3524 and 81.64% within-paper pairwise accuracy, a 59% relative error reduction over the best LLM-as-judge baseline. This work addresses a critical gap in automated peer review by providing a method that aligns figure quality with manuscript evidence, unlike traditional IQA or CLIP-based methods. It could improve the consistency and efficiency of scientific figure assessment in publishing and AI-for-science applications. SciFigAlign fine-tunes CLIP and SciBERT end-to-end with per-modality cross-attention and CubeMLP fusion, jointly optimizing SmoothL1 regression with a within-paper ranking hinge loss. Ablations show that manuscript-grounded inputs, citing-context denoising, and ranking supervision are all critical for performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Scientific figure assessment differs from general image quality evaluation because figures must be visually legible, support the manuscript's claims, and communicate evidence clearly. SciFigAlign addresses these limitations by grounding figure quality in manuscript evidence through fine-tuning.

<a id="item-215"></a>
## [Visual Credit Audit Reveals Unsupported Correct Answers in Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27069) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Visual Credit Audit (VCA), a novel evaluation framework that separates whether a multimodal LLM's correct answer is genuinely supported by visual evidence or merely by text-only or blank contexts. Across four open MLLMs and two spatial benchmarks, VCA finds that 12.73-26.25% of correct decisions are uncredited, meaning they lack visual support. This matters because it exposes a significant flaw in existing benchmark evaluations: many correct answers may be achieved without using the image, undermining the validity of spatial reasoning benchmarks. VCA provides a training- and label-free method to audit model performance, which could lead to more reliable evaluation of multimodal AI systems and guide improvements in model design. VCA introduces dependence-credited correctness (D-CC), which measures whether the image adds support beyond controls. Matched same-split image permutation reduces D-CC by 21.25-47.80 points, with all paired 95% intervals above zero, confirming that null controls cannot identify relation response. The study also uses fixed-pixel relation contrasts and a 3x3 evidence-source factorial to show why null controls fail.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) are designed to reason over both text and images, and benchmarks like spatial reasoning tasks evaluate their ability to use visual information. However, closed yes/no benchmarks can reward correct answers even when the image provides little additional support over text-only or blank contexts, leading to inflated performance estimates. VCA addresses this by auditing whether the model's decision is actually supported by the image, using statistical controls and permutation tests.

## [Lightweight ACA Adapts CT Foundation Models for Anatomy-Level Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27154) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Anatomy Contextualized Adaptation (ACA), a lightweight method that adapts frozen CT foundation models for anatomy-level vision-language alignment while preserving global context. ACA uses TotalSegmentator to decompose CT volumes into anatomy-level embeddings, refined by a transformer, and aligns them with per-anatomy and scan-level text from radiology reports. This method addresses a key limitation of whole-volume CT foundation models, which dilute fine-grained anatomical signals, and offers a computationally efficient alternative to training fine-grained models from scratch. It could improve zero-shot finding classification in medical imaging, benefiting downstream tasks like disease detection and report generation. ACA consistently outperforms frozen foundation model baselines and existing fine-grained methods on Merlin and CT-RATE benchmarks for zero-shot finding classification, requiring less than one hour of training once embeddings are cached. The attention weights learned by ACA's inter-anatomy transformer indicate plausible cross-anatomy context routing.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: CT vision-language foundation models are pre-trained on large volumes of CT scans and radiology reports to learn joint representations, but they often use whole-volume representations that miss fine-grained anatomical details. Fine-grained vision-language pre-training (FVLP) aligns anatomy-level features with text but discards global context and is computationally expensive. TotalSegmentator is a tool that automatically segments over 100 anatomical structures in CT images, enabling anatomy-level analysis. ACA builds on these concepts by adapting frozen foundation models with lightweight modules to achieve both fine-grained alignment and global contextualization.

## [Hybrid LLM/SLM System Boosts E-Commerce Item Discoverability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27172) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a scalable discovery-augmented search system for e-commerce that uses intent-conditioned recall expansion, combining closed-weight LLMs for head queries with a finetuned SLM for tail queries. The system extends discovery coverage from about 60% to 80% of query traffic at roughly 30% of the teacher model's inference cost. This approach addresses a critical cost-quality tradeoff in generative retrieval, making it feasible to deploy advanced discovery capabilities in large-scale marketplaces. It also highlights a potential marketplace-balancing effect, giving long-tail and emerging products more exposure, which could reshape e-commerce search and recommendation strategies. The system uses a two-stage hybrid architecture: LLMs maximize discoverability for head queries, while a finetuned SLM (trained via LoRA adapters and teacher-student distillation) handles tail queries. Evaluation uses a dual framework: LLM-as-a-judge metrics validated against human preferences, and end-to-end session-level purchase analysis.

**Background**: Traditional e-commerce search systems prioritize precision over recall, often missing substitute, complementary, or thematically related items. Intent-conditioned recall expansion generates implicit user intents to expand candidate recall while maintaining relevance. Hybrid LLM/SLM architectures are emerging as a practical pattern to balance cost and quality, with SLMs handling routine tasks and LLMs reserved for complex ones.

## [OphthaReason and MM-Retinal-Reason: Advancing Ophthalmic Multimodal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16129) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MM-Retinal-Reason, the first ophthalmic multimodal dataset covering both basic and complex reasoning tasks, and OphthaReason, the first ophthalmology-specific multimodal reasoning model with step-by-step reasoning traces. The model achieves state-of-the-art performance, outperforming general-purpose and medical MLLMs by at least 15% to 25%. This work bridges the gap between basic visual reasoning and complex clinical reasoning in ophthalmology, which is crucial for real-world AI-assisted diagnosis. It sets a new benchmark for medical multimodal reasoning and could inspire similar efforts in other medical specialties. The model uses a novel Uncertainty-Aware Dynamic Thinking (UADT) method that estimates sample-level uncertainty via entropy and dynamically adjusts reasoning depth using a shaped advantage mechanism. The dataset and model are publicly available on Hugging Face and GitHub, with an initial subset of the dataset released.

## [Hybrid Model Balances Centralized and Distributed Control in Morphogenesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.10101) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a hybrid model that couples a compact convolutional neural network controller with a Gray-Scott reaction-diffusion substrate, achieving 100% strict convergence in about 165 steps, whereas pure RD and NN-dominant baselines failed to converge within the same horizon. The hybrid approach uses approximately 15 times less L1 effort and over 200 times less L2 power than the NN-dominant controller. This work provides a quantitative model for controlled self-organization, addressing a fundamental question in embodied intelligence and developmental biology about the division of labor between centralized guidance and distributed dynamics. The findings could inform the design of more efficient and robust control strategies in robotics and AI, where balancing top-down control with self-organization is crucial. The controller observes the U and V fields and applies smooth gain-scheduled modulations to the feed and kill parameters (ΔF and ΔK). Moderate amplitudes (A ≈ 0.03–0.045) form a 'Goldilocks zone' with 100% quasi-convergence in 94–96 steps, illustrating the 'seed then cede' principle.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Embodied morphogenesis relies on a division of labor between centralized guidance and distributed material dynamics. Reaction-diffusion systems, such as the Gray-Scott model, can generate complex patterns from simple local interactions, while neural networks provide centralized control. Gain scheduling is a control technique that adjusts controller gains based on operating conditions, which is used here to modulate the reaction-diffusion parameters.

## [BioPro: Training-Free Difference-Aware Gender Fairness for VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.00807) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces BioPro (Bias Orthogonal Projection), a training-free framework for difference-aware gender fairness in vision-language models (VLMs). It selectively debiases neutral contexts while preserving legitimate group-specific attributes in explicit ones, and demonstrates generalization to continuous bias variables like scene brightness. This work addresses a critical gap in VLM fairness by distinguishing between contexts requiring neutrality and those where group-specific attributes are legitimate. It offers a practical, training-free solution that can be applied to existing models, potentially influencing future fairness interventions in multimodal AI systems. BioPro identifies a low-dimensional gender-variation subspace using counterfactual embeddings and applies orthogonal projection to selectively neutralize gender-related information. Experiments show it reduces gender bias in neutral cases while maintaining gender faithfulness in explicit ones, and it also generalizes to continuous bias variables such as scene brightness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) inherit social biases from training data, particularly in gender representation. Traditional fairness interventions often enforce uniform treatment across groups, failing to distinguish between contexts where neutrality is required and those where group-specific attributes are legitimate. Difference-aware fairness, previously explored in text-only models, extends this concept to multimodal domains, advocating for selective debiasing. Training-free debiasing frameworks operate during inference without additional training, making them compatible with existing models.

## [AdaMARP: Adaptive Multi-Agent Framework for Immersive Role-Playing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.11007) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AdaMARP introduces an adaptive multi-agent interaction framework for LLM-based role-playing, featuring an immersive message format that interleaves [Thought], (Action), Environment, and Speech, along with an explicit Scene Manager that controls role-playing via discrete actions (init_scene, pick_speaker, switch_scene, add_role, end) with rationales. The framework is trained on newly constructed datasets (AdaRPSet and AdaSMSet) and evaluated on a new trajectory-level benchmark called AdaptiveBench. This work addresses a recognized limitation in LLM role-playing systems: the lack of dynamic scene handling and multi-character orchestration. By enabling adaptive scene transitions and on-the-fly character introduction, AdaMARP could significantly enhance the immersion and interactivity of AI-driven narratives, benefiting applications in gaming, virtual assistants, and interactive storytelling. Experiments across multiple backbones and model scales show consistent improvements: AdaRPSet enhances character consistency, environment grounding, and narrative coherence, with an 8B actor outperforming several commercial LLMs, while AdaSMSet enables smoother scene transitions and more natural role introductions, surpassing Claude Sonnet 4.5 using only a 14B LLM. The code is currently under internal review and will be released later.

AdaMARP formalizes adaptive role-playing, where the system dynamically selects the next speaker, switches scenes as the plot progresses, and introduces new characters when needed. The framework uses a Scene Manager to orchestrate these discrete actions, and the new benchmark AdaptiveBench evaluates trajectory-level performance, going beyond final-answer accuracy.

Experiments show binary detection is robust, but distinguishing offensive from hateful content remains challenging in multi-class settings due to label ambiguity and dataset imbalance.

## [LLM Memory Length Shapes Cooperation in Social Particle Swarm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.12250) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This study extends the Social Particle Swarm (SPS) model by replacing rule-based agents with LLM agents (using Gemini 2.0 Flash) endowed with Big Five personality scores and varying memory lengths, and finds that memory length is a critical parameter governing collective behavior. Even minimal memory drastically suppresses cooperation, transitioning the system from stable cooperative clusters to scattered defection as memory length increases. This work provides insights into how memory affects emergent social behavior in Generative Agent-Based Modeling (GABM), which is crucial for designing more realistic multi-agent simulations and understanding cooperation dynamics in AI systems. It also highlights the importance of memory interpretation in LLM agents, potentially impacting fields like social science research and multi-agent AI development. The study used Gemini 2.0 Flash and found that Big Five personality traits correlated with agent behaviors in partial agreement with human experiments, supporting model validity. Sentiment analysis of agents' reasoning texts showed that the model interprets memory increasingly negatively as its length grows, providing a micro-level account of cooperation suppression.

In this model, agents play the Prisoner's Dilemma with neighbors, where each rational agent has a dominant strategy to defect, yet mutual defection leaves both worse off than mutual cooperation. This paper conceptualizes the ABPMS process frame as a hybrid business process representation that combines semi-concurrently executed procedural and declarative process models, extending the open-world assumption to procedural models. It also analyzes how procedural behaviors manifest as Declare constraints, laying groundwork for new process discovery techniques. This hybrid conceptualization addresses a research gap in AI-augmented business process management, potentially enabling more flexible and autonomous process execution. The paper extends the open-world assumption of declarative models to procedural models, allowing any set of non-conflicting models to be combined for execution. It provides an in-depth analysis of how procedural behaviors correspond to sets of discovered Declare constraints, each tied to a specific type of eventually-follows relation, revealing behavioral overlaps between the two paradigms.

## [ATOD: Hybrid Annealed Distillation and RL for Multi-Turn Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27814) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ATOD (Annealed Turn-aware On-policy Distillation), a hybrid online distillation algorithm that combines on-policy distillation (OPD) and reinforcement learning (RL) with an annealed schedule and turn-level reweighting. Experiments on ALFWorld, WebShop, and Search-QA show ATOD outperforms OPD and GRPO baselines, improving average success rate by 4.16 points over OPD and 23.62 points over GRPO. This work addresses a known limitation of on-policy distillation—performance saturation near the teacher—by explicitly combining it with RL, which can push beyond teacher-level performance. The approach could improve training efficiency and final performance for small language-model agents in long-horizon interactive tasks, potentially impacting the broader agentic AI ecosystem. ATOD uses an annealed OPD-RL schedule where OPD dominates early training and RL is gradually strengthened. It also introduces Turn-level Disagreement-Uncertainty Reweighting (T-DUR) to amplify high-utility turns and improve dense supervision in long trajectories. Across three student sizes, ATOD surpasses the corresponding teacher models by 2.16 points on average.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation (OPD) is a knowledge distillation technique where the student model generates its own trajectories and learns from teacher feedback, providing dense guidance but often plateauing near the teacher's performance. Reinforcement learning (RL) directly optimizes environment rewards, enabling exploration beyond the teacher but suffering from sparse and delayed feedback. ATOD combines these complementary strengths through an annealed schedule and turn-level reweighting to improve training efficiency and final performance for multi-turn agentic tasks.

## [FirstResearch: Making LLM-Proposed Research Questions Auditable](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05682) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FirstResearch introduces a framework that generates structured Research Question Certificates, making LLM-proposed scientific research questions auditable and falsifiable. In evaluations, it outperformed baselines inspired by AI co-scientist, Agent Laboratory, and AI Scientist-v2, scoring 4.86/5 under a Gemini-2.5-Flash judge. This addresses a critical gap in AI-driven scientific discovery: the lack of auditability in LLM-generated research questions. By making the reasoning behind a research question explicit, it enhances reproducibility and trust in AI-assisted research, potentially accelerating scientific progress while maintaining rigor. The certificate records primitive definitions, assumptions, a mechanism model, a tension or contradiction, a falsifiable hypothesis, a minimal decisive test, and a failure update rule. Ablation studies show that removing certificates drops scores below 1/5 under both DeepSeek and Gemini judges, highlighting the certificate's central role.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: LLM systems for scientific discovery increasingly assist with ideation, literature synthesis, and experiment planning, but the initial research questions they propose often lack transparency. Falsifiability, a concept introduced by Karl Popper, requires that a hypothesis be testable and disprovable, which is essential for scientific rigor. FirstResearch builds on this by structuring the question formation process to ensure each component is inspectable before execution.

## [Questioning Adaptive Global Spatial Attention in Traffic Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12462) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper systematically compares adaptive global spatial attention with simple uniform full-range mixing across six traffic benchmarks, finding no consistent advantage for attention and relative MAE changes from -1.58% to +1.26%. The study shows that uniform mixing reduces node-scale spatial-mixing complexity from O(N^2) to O(N). This challenges a common assumption in traffic forecasting that complex adaptive attention is necessary for capturing global spatial dependencies. The findings could lead to simpler and more efficient models, reducing computational costs without sacrificing accuracy, which is significant for real-time traffic forecasting applications. The paper designs a controlled ablation framework that replaces only the spatial mixing module to isolate the effect of attention. It also proposes a hypothesized model decomposing spatial attention into a row-uniform global background and a non-uniform residual, showing dataset-dependent effects of the residual.

Agent-UCT extends the UCT algorithm with a reuse-aware regularization term derived from a bipartite prefix reuse graph, enabling cost-aware optimization of agentic workflows like RAG pipelines. The framework includes RAGSpace, a unified five-dimensional configuration space, and WTB, a test bench with deterministic replay and caching. This work addresses the combinatorial explosion in optimizing agentic workflows under tight evaluation budgets, potentially reducing redundant computation significantly. It offers a principled approach that could improve the efficiency and cost-effectiveness of LLM-based systems in production. Experiments on HotpotQA and UltraDomain show Agent-UCT identifies configurations with the highest out-of-sample performance among fixed framework presets. Bipartite prefix reuse reduces logical search cost by 73.6% relative to the no-prefix-sharing upper bound, and sampling-based evaluation achieves a 4.2x wall-clock speedup over full-pool evaluation.

## [TRACE-CTI: Auditable Governance Framework for TTP Claims](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24563) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TRACE-CTI is a post-extraction claim-governance framework that preserves run-level Predictions, aggregates them into configuration-level GraphAssertions, materializes setup-deduplicated corroboration as ConsensusAssertions, and exposes only GraphAssertions backed by policy-compliant validation grounds. It was evaluated on two public CTI corpora comprising 65 reports and 5,303 sentences, using a controlled 2x3 matrix of retrievers and generator families, incrementally ingested across six GraphVersions. This framework addresses the critical issue of trustworthiness in automated threat intelligence mapping to MITRE ATT&CK, which is increasingly relied upon by Security Operations Centers. By providing explicit, auditable governance of extracted TTP claims, it enables security analysts to make informed trust decisions, potentially improving the reliability of threat detection and response. The evaluation showed that increasing setup support from k>=1 to six-setup unanimity raised gold-aligned precision from 25.3% to 90.6%, while recall decreased from 88.2% to 16.3%. The graph also directly answers seven questions about provenance, trust, versioning, dependency, disagreement, and review-queue that the evaluated minimal flat output cannot fully answer without enrichment or reprocessing.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Cyber Threat Intelligence (CTI) describes cyber threats, actors, tools, and behaviors observed during attacks, often published in threat reports. Security Operations Centers increasingly use automated mapping of CTI reports to MITRE ATT&CK, but extractor outputs can be fallible and lack the evidence and provenance needed for trust decisions. TRACE-CTI is a framework that manages the lifecycle of these extractions, distinguishing between model-generated observations and trusted intelligence.

## [Awareness interventions fail to curb AI sycophancy's persuasive power](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25166) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Two preregistered experiments (n=940 and n=650) tested whether warnings or observational videos about AI sycophancy could mitigate its effects. Both interventions changed user perceptions—reducing perceived objectivity or enjoyment—but neither reduced the AI's persuasiveness, and a pooled analysis of six interventions (n=3,982) confirmed this pattern. This research challenges the assumption that individual-level interventions like warning labels or AI literacy can protect users from AI harms. It highlights the need for systemic solutions to address sycophancy, which can entrench attitudes and amplify misinformation. The warning reduced the AI's perceived objectivity, while the video reduced enjoyment, mediated by a reduced belief that validation was uniquely earned. None of the six interventions reduced persuasiveness, suggesting that awareness alone is insufficient.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: AI sycophancy refers to when AI systems uncritically echo user assertions, favoring affirmation over evidence, often due to training methods like RLHF. Users often fail to recognize this behavior, a phenomenon called 'sycophancy blindness,' which can lead to entrenched attitudes and reduced trust in information.

## [New Results Improve Approximate EFX Allocations to 2/3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12413) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proves the existence of 2/3-EFX allocations for settings with at most seven agents, three-valued additive valuations, or multigraph-representable valuations, improving upon the previous best guarantee of 0.618-EFX. These results advance the theoretical frontier of fair division, showing that better approximation guarantees are achievable in broader settings than previously known. This narrows the gap toward resolving the central open problem of whether exact EFX allocations always exist. The paper also provides polynomial-time algorithms for some of these cases, contrasting with prior algorithms for exact EFX that may be non-polynomial. The results are interpreted both as generalizations of exact EFX existence and as breaking the 0.618 barrier under restricted settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: EFX (envy-freeness up to any good) is a fairness notion in discrete fair division where no agent envies another after removing any single good from the other's bundle. The existence of EFX allocations is a fundamental open problem; known results include exact EFX for up to three agents, two-valued valuations, or graph-representable valuations. Approximate EFX (α-EFX) relaxes the condition, and prior work guaranteed 0.618-EFX for any number of agents with additive valuations.

A new study evaluated Google Gemini 2.0 Flash and OpenAI ChatGPT-4o across 52 clinical scenarios, finding both models returned identical diagnoses across rephrased variants (100% consistency) but changed diagnoses when irrelevant details were added (Gemini 40.0%, ChatGPT 30.0%). This study highlights that high consistency under rephrasing does not guarantee resistance to irrelevant manipulation or appropriate context use, underscoring the need for clinician oversight and structured safeguards before LLMs are deployed in medical diagnosis. ChatGPT responded to added clinical context more often than Gemini (77.8% vs. 55.6%), but a larger share of its changes were clinically inappropriate (33.3% vs. 22.2%). ## [FPEdit: Robust LLM Fingerprinting via Localized Parameter Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.02092) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FPEdit introduces a novel framework for LLM fingerprinting that uses knowledge editing to inject semantically coherent natural language triggers through sparse, targeted modifications to model weights. It employs Promote-Suppress Value Vector Optimization to enhance target token likelihood while suppressing competitors, achieving 94-100% fingerprint retention under fine-tuning and parameter-efficient adaptation. This work addresses a fundamental trade-off in LLM fingerprinting between intrinsic methods (requiring full parameter access) and backdoor methods (using statistically anomalous triggers). FPEdit is the first approach to simultaneously achieve robustness against adaptation, resistance to detection, and preservation of model utility, providing a minimally invasive solution for provenance verification in adversarial deployment scenarios. FPEdit can embed 10 fingerprint pairs into LLaMA2-7B in under 2 minutes using less than 30 GB of GPU memory, a substantial reduction in resource requirements. It remains robust under quantization, pruning, and stochastic decoding, and preserves performance on downstream benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

Fingerprinting is a technique to embed verifiable signatures into models to prove ownership. Traditional methods either require full parameter access (intrinsic) or use statistically anomalous triggers (backdoor) that can be easily detected. Knowledge editing is a rising technique for efficiently updating factual knowledge in LLMs with minimal parameter alteration, which FPEdit leverages for fingerprint injection.

## [Privacy-Preserving Music Retrieval via Additive Homomorphic Encryption](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07044) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes using additive homomorphic encryption (AHE) for privacy-preserving music information retrieval, demonstrating that similarity search can be performed efficiently when only one operand is encrypted. The authors implement two music-specific inference attacks, introduce structure-aware additive primitives, and evaluate their approach across four audio datasets. This work addresses a practical limitation of fully homomorphic encryption (FHE) by showing that additive homomorphic encryption can achieve privacy-preserving music retrieval with significantly lower computational overhead. It provides a viable path for deploying privacy-preserving machine learning in music retrieval systems, balancing privacy and efficiency. The approach leverages Paillier or CKKS restricted to additive operations, avoiding ciphertext-ciphertext multiplication and bootstrapping. The authors show that additive search preserves nearest-neighbor rankings exactly while scaling better in embedding dimension than a true-additive Paillier baseline, and they introduce learned per-block weighting at no extra cryptographic cost.

**Background**: Homomorphic encryption allows computations on encrypted data without decryption. Fully homomorphic encryption (FHE) supports arbitrary computations but is impractical at scale due to high overhead. Additive homomorphic encryption (AHE), such as Paillier, supports only addition, but is much more efficient. In music retrieval, embeddings are used for similarity search, and privacy concerns arise when sharing these embeddings.

This work addresses a critical yet often overlooked problem in mobile manipulation: where the robot should stand to grasp an object. By providing a data-efficient, geometry-aware approach that avoids full task-and-motion optimization, it could enable faster and more reliable grasping in real-world applications, advancing the deployment of mobile manipulators in dynamic environments. The model uses a PointNet++ style point cloud encoder with an MLP to score dense grids of candidate poses, enabling rapid online selection. The two-stage curriculum combines cheap heuristics for coverage with targeted simulation for calibration, and the method degrades gracefully when wrong, choosing safer and more reachable stances.

**Background**: In robotic grasping, the base placement of a mobile manipulator significantly affects the success and safety of the task. GBPP leverages a two-stage curriculum learning approach, where a simple rule generates a large labeled dataset, and high-fidelity simulation refines the model, balancing data efficiency and accuracy. PointNet++ is a widely used neural network architecture for processing 3D point clouds, capable of learning hierarchical features from unordered point sets.

## [AMEND++ Benchmark Predicts Clinical Trial Eligibility Criteria Amendments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.06300) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces AMEND++, a benchmark suite for the novel NLP task of eligibility criteria amendment prediction, along with CAMLM, a revision-aware pretraining strategy. It includes two datasets: AMEND and AMEND_LLM, the latter refined via an LLM-based denoising pipeline. Clinical trial amendments cause delays, increased costs, and administrative burden, with eligibility criteria being the most amended component. This benchmark enables forecasting of such amendments, potentially leading to more robust and cost-effective trial design, benefiting the healthcare AI and clinical NLP communities. CAMLM leverages historical edits to learn amendment-sensitive representations, unlike standard masked language modeling that treats all tokens equally. Experiments across diverse baselines show CAMLM consistently improves amendment prediction.

## [Context Shapes Truth Representations in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.06599) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper systematically studies how truth vectors in LLMs change when context is introduced, measuring directional change and magnitude across four models and four datasets. It finds that context generally amplifies the separation between true and false representations, and that larger models distinguish relevant context via directional changes while smaller models use magnitude differences. This work provides a geometric characterization of how context transforms truth representations, which is crucial for interpretability and alignment research. Understanding these dynamics could lead to better methods for detecting and mitigating hallucinations in LLMs. The study measures directional change (theta) and relative magnitude of truth vectors across layers, finding that truth vectors are roughly orthogonal in early layers, converge in middle layers, and may stabilize or increase in later layers. Context conflicting with parametric knowledge produces larger geometric changes than aligned context.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) encode the truthfulness of statements as linear features in their residual stream activations, often called 'truth vectors' or 'truth directions'. Prior work, such as 'The Geometry of Truth', has shown that these vectors can classify truthfulness reliably. This paper extends that line of research by examining how these representations change when context is added, which is important for understanding how LLMs integrate contextual information with parametric knowledge.

## [LLM Pipeline Makes Implicit Premises Explicit in Enthymemes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.06114) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a pipeline that uses two large language models (LLMs) and a neuro-symbolic reasoner based on a SAT solver to make implicit premises in enthymemes explicit and verify logical entailment. The pipeline is evaluated on two enthymeme datasets, showing promising performance in selecting the correct implicit premise. This work bridges the gap between NLP methods, which can identify enthymemes but not decode their logic, and logic-based approaches that require a pre-existing knowledge base. By integrating LLMs with formal reasoning, it offers a systematic method for translating textual arguments into logical form, potentially advancing argument mining and reasoning systems. The pipeline consists of three components: an LLM generates intermediate implicit premises from the explicit premise and claim; a second LLM translates natural language into logical formulas; and a neuro-symbolic reasoner based on a SAT solver determines entailment. The evaluation uses precision, recall, F1-score, and accuracy metrics on two enthymeme datasets.

Neuro-symbolic AI combines neural networks with symbolic reasoning to improve reliability and explainability.

## [MICA: Critic-Free RL for Long-Horizon Emotional Support Dialogue](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.06194) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MICA, a critic-free reinforcement learning framework that uses multi-granularity intertemporal credit assignment to improve per-turn credit assignment in long-horizon emotional support dialogues. It consistently outperforms GRPO and REINFORCE++ on benchmarks like EMPA, EQ-Bench, and EmoBench, achieving up to +42.5 on EMPA without additional rollout cost. This work addresses a critical challenge in applying RL to multi-turn LLM interactions, where sparse rewards and poor credit assignment hinder performance. By enabling effective turn-aware credit assignment without a critic, MICA offers a practical and scalable solution for training interactive dialogue systems, potentially benefiting mental health support and other long-horizon conversational AI applications. MICA derives both immediate and delayed credit from a shared potential function over the user's structured support state, using Incremental Distance Reward for per-turn effects and Monte Carlo returns for delayed effects. It employs scope-specific normalization to form a mixed advantage, avoiding matched-state comparisons, rollout trees, or learned critics, and remains robust to reward judges.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) for large language models (LLMs) has shown promise in single-turn tasks, but multi-turn interactions pose challenges due to sparse rewards and difficulty in assigning credit to each turn. In emotional support dialogues, responses influence future user states, making matched-state step-wise comparison infeasible, while trajectory-level supervision is insufficient. Existing RL algorithms like PPO use a critic network to estimate advantages, but critic-free methods like GRPO and REINFORCE++ have emerged to reduce computational overhead. MICA builds on this trend by providing a critic-free approach specifically designed for multi-turn emotional support tasks.

## [EyExIn: Anchoring Retinal VLMs with Deep Expert Injection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.07131) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces EyExIn, a data-efficient framework that injects domain-specific expert knowledge into retinal vision-language models via a Deep Expert Injection mechanism. It employs an Expert-Aware Dual-Stream encoding strategy and a Semantic-Adaptive Gated Fusion module to bridge the perception and reasoning gaps, achieving state-of-the-art precision in ophthalmic visual question answering. This work addresses critical limitations in applying large vision-language models to medical imaging, specifically ophthalmology, where domain-specific knowledge is essential for reliable diagnosis. By improving the grounding of visual evidence and reducing hallucinations, EyExIn could enhance the trustworthiness and clinical adoption of automated ophthalmic AI systems. EyExIn uses an Expert-Aware Dual-Stream encoding that separates general anatomical context from specialized pathological semantics, and a Semantic-Adaptive Gated Fusion module to amplify subtle lesion signals while filtering noise. The Adaptive Deep Expert Injection embeds fused visual features as residual biases into intermediate LLM layers, creating persistent 'Vision Anchors' that keep reasoning grounded in visual evidence. Extensive experiments across four benchmarks show consistent outperformance over massive proprietary systems.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Large Vision Language Models (LVLMs) combine visual encoders with large language models to perform tasks like visual question answering. In medical imaging, they face two structural deficiencies: the Perception Gap, where general-purpose encoders miss fine-grained pathological cues, and the Reasoning Gap, where language priors override sparse visual evidence in deeper layers, leading to hallucinations. EyExIn addresses these by injecting expert knowledge directly into the model's reasoning stack.

## [Gated Adaptation Reduces Forgetting in Human Activity Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.10046) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a parameter-efficient continual learning framework that uses channel-wise gated modulation of frozen pretrained representations to mitigate catastrophic forgetting in domain-incremental human activity recognition (HAR). On PAMAP2 with 8 sequential subjects, the method reduces forgetting from 39.7% to 16.2% and improves final accuracy from 56.7% to 77.7%, while training less than 2% of parameters. This work addresses a critical challenge in on-device AI for IoT and wearable sensors, where models must adapt to new users without forgetting previous ones and without transmitting sensitive data to the cloud. The parameter-efficient approach enables continual learning in resource-constrained environments, potentially improving applications like remote health monitoring and smart home automation. The method restricts learned transformations to diagonal scaling of existing features, preserving the geometry of pretrained representations while enabling subject-specific modulation. Theoretical analysis shows that gating implements a bounded diagonal operator that limits representational drift compared to unconstrained linear transformations, and the approach matches or exceeds standard continual learning baselines without replay buffers or task-specific regularization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Continual learning aims to enable models to learn new tasks sequentially without forgetting previous ones, a challenge known as catastrophic forgetting. In domain-incremental learning, the label space remains fixed while the input distribution shifts, such as when adapting to new subjects with different movement patterns. Channel-wise gating is a technique that selectively modulates feature maps, and freezing pretrained representations is a common strategy to reduce forgetting.

GroupRAG, a cognitively inspired framework, identifies latent structural groups within a problem and performs retrieval and reasoning from multiple conceptual starting points. This paper introduces a novel approach that integrates facial-expression-aware signals into LLM-based tutoring systems via prompt-level conditioning, without end-to-end retraining. Across 960 multi-turn conversations with three tutor backbones (GPT-5.1, Claude Opus 4.5, Gemini 2.5 Pro), AU-based conditioning consistently improved empathetic responsiveness. This work addresses a critical gap in AI tutoring by leveraging facial expressions—a practical cue of learner confusion, frustration, or engagement—to enhance empathy. It demonstrates a lightweight, scalable method that improves tutor responsiveness without compromising pedagogical clarity, potentially advancing affective computing in education. The study compared four tutor variants: text-only baseline, multimodal baseline with random facial frame, and two AUM-based methods (textual AU descriptions or peak-expression frame selection). Control analyses confirmed that improvements did not come at the cost of pedagogical clarity or responsiveness to textual cues, though textual AU abstraction and peak-frame injection showed model-dependent advantages.

## [VistaHop: New Benchmark for Long-Horizon Visual DeepSearch](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03273) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VistaHop is a new benchmark introduced to evaluate multimodal large language models (MLLMs) on Visual DeepSearch tasks, which require iterative image inspection and multi-step reasoning. It includes 600 images, 25 visual search scenarios, and 600 tasks, along with a unified evaluation framework called VistaArena. Existing benchmarks mostly evaluate single-step visual understanding or isolated query responses, failing to test long-horizon, multi-step reasoning. VistaHop fills this gap and reveals that even state-of-the-art models like SenseNova-MARS-32B achieve only 26.33% Pass@1, highlighting the need for improved agentic methods. VistaHop evaluates repeated image inspection, visual-anchor grounding, and long-horizon evidence traversal across different visual regions. The best-performing model, SenseNova-MARS-32B, achieved only 26.33% Pass@1, indicating significant room for improvement.

## [Audit Finds Simple Linear Encoder Nearly Optimal for Multi-Channel Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04752) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new empirical audit of eight input encoders for multi-channel signal transformers finds that the standard per-channel linear projection performs nearly as well as more complex alternatives, due to spontaneous orthogonalization. The study, posted on arXiv (2606.04752), evaluates encoders on a synthetic benchmark and the ETTh1 dataset using next-step negative log-likelihood. This finding provides practical guidance for ML practitioners, suggesting that the default per-channel linear projection is sufficient for most tasks, potentially simplifying model design and reducing computational overhead. It also offers a surprising insight into the implicit regularization of transformers, which could influence future research on architectural choices. The audit covers eight encoders, including a shared-scalar baseline, per-channel linear projections, an orthogonality regularizer, a nonlinear MLP, block-partitioned concatenation, channel-independent and channel-as-token architectures, and a projected positional encoding. The shared-scalar baseline collapses due to information-theoretic reasons, and the channel-independent PatchTST-spirit baseline overfits on the synthetic benchmark and underperforms on both. Paired tests show that projecting sinusoidal positional encoding through a learned linear layer edges others at small C, while a nonlinear MLP stem edges them at the largest C.

**Background**: Transformers for multi-channel scalar signals need to embed multiple simultaneous values into a single vector per time step. The study uses a synthetic benchmark where channel identity is informative and the ETTh1 dataset, a real-world electricity transformer dataset, to evaluate next-step prediction performance.

FCGraft, a Functional Cache Grafting framework, was introduced to improve CodeLLM-based policy synthesis for embodied agents. It caches and grafts function-level KV caches, achieving 18.31% higher task success rate and 2.3x faster policy synthesis compared to RAGCache. This addresses two critical limitations in CodeLLM-based policy generation: high prefill latency and robustness issues from fully generative decoding. By reusing validated code skeletons, FCGraft can significantly speed up and stabilize policy synthesis, potentially accelerating real-world robot deployment. FCGraft maintains a library of function-level validated code skeletons with associated prompt-level KV caches. It uses stitching to compose cached function segments into a composite policy and patching to locally adapt code regions for task-specific parameters, minimizing additional decoding.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: CodeLLMs generate executable code policies for embodied agents by translating natural language goals into control programs. However, they suffer from repetitive prefill computation over long prompts and robustness issues due to fully generative decoding. KV cache caching is a technique to reuse intermediate Transformer states to reduce computation, and FCGraft extends this to function-level code skeletons.

<a id="item-255"></a>
## [RWGBench: New Benchmark for Citation-Centric Related Work Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24894) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RWGBench is a new benchmark introduced to evaluate related work generation (RWG) in large language models, focusing on citation decision-making rather than text similarity. It includes a test set of 100 curated computer science papers and a retrieval corpus of 1.09 million documents, with a multi-dimensional evaluation framework covering citation selection, contextual appropriateness, organization, and discourse structure. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in RWG evaluation, as traditional metrics based on lexical or semantic similarity fail to capture academically important failures like inappropriate citation selection. By providing a citation-centric testbed, RWGBench can drive the development of RWG systems that better align with scholarly writing practices, benefiting researchers and AI developers in scientific writing automation. The benchmark is constructed from a large-scale collection of 40,108 computer science papers and a retrieval corpus of 1.09 million documents. Experiments reveal systematic limitations in current systems that are obscured by standard evaluations, and Oracle studies disentangle retrieval-level and generation-level bottlenecks; human evaluation shows that citation-centric metrics align substantially better with expert judgment than surface-level text metrics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Related work generation is a task where LLMs produce the related work section of a scientific paper. Traditionally, evaluation has relied on summarization-oriented metrics that compare generated text to reference sections using lexical or semantic similarity. However, related work writing is fundamentally a citation-level scholarly positioning task, requiring the selection, organization, and framing of prior work to clarify how a target paper relates to and differs from existing research. RWGBench shifts the evaluation focus to citation decision-making, aiming to better capture the academic quality of generated related work.

## [Adversarial Pragmatics Framework and Seed Benchmark for AI Safety Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01153) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a diagnostic framework and an 18-item seed benchmark for evaluating language models under adversarial pragmatic conditions, such as instruction conflict and indirect speech acts. It also includes a 54-row pilot and a six-cell LLM-judge assessment, with a protocol that separates task success, policy compliance, risk, refusal, attribution, and confidence. This work addresses a critical gap in AI safety evaluation by moving beyond pass/fail labels to diagnose the underlying causes of model failures, such as capability limits versus policy ambiguity. It provides concrete tools for more granular safety assessments, which could improve the reliability of AI systems in real-world applications. The benchmark separates four inference targets that a single label can conceal: regime-relative reference, configured-system behaviour, evaluator-output interpretation, and taxonomic assignment. The pilot results show that an LLM judge that graded its own outputs with the expected answer visible missed safety-relevant minority classes, and hierarchical pooling shrank the rubric effect toward the group mean.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Adversarial pragmatics refers to safety-relevant model behaviour in cases involving instruction conflict, embedded commands, quotation, scope ambiguity, deixis, and indirect speech acts. Existing safety benchmarks often compress these nuanced behaviours into binary labels, obscuring the reasons for failures. This framework aims to provide a more diagnostic approach, extending to multi-turn agent transcripts, though the seed set only includes a single-turn tool-result contrast.

## [Auto-AEG: Scalable Data Pipeline for Open-Vocabulary Audio Event Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04383) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Auto-AEG introduces a scalable pipeline that automatically constructs supervision data for open-vocabulary audio event grounding, combining synthetic clips with exact intervals and pseudo-labels from real audio for reinforcement learning. On the new AEGBench benchmark, it achieves +73.9% and +23.1% mIoU improvements over zero-shot baselines. This work addresses a critical data scarcity bottleneck in open-vocabulary audio event grounding, a task that bridges large audio-language models and classical sound event detection. By demonstrating that automatically constructed data can significantly enhance temporal localization, it paves the way for more precise and scalable audio-language systems. The pipeline uses programmatically synthesized clips with exact ground-truth intervals for cold-start supervised training, and multi-model pseudo-labels on real-world audio to provide reward signals for reinforcement learning. The authors release AEGBench, a difficulty-stratified benchmark, and show that gains generalize to held-out SED and other audio grounding benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Large Audio-Language Models (LALMs) can reason about audio but struggle with precise temporal localization, while classical Sound Event Detection (SED) achieves frame-level precision only for a closed set of labels. Open-vocabulary audio event grounding aims to predict all time intervals for a sound event described by arbitrary natural language, but lacks large-scale supervision data. Pseudo-labeling is a semi-supervised technique that uses model predictions as labels for unlabeled data, which Auto-AEG leverages to scale data construction.

## [G2VD: Causal Framework Boosts AI-Generated Video Detection Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04607) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces G2VD, a novel framework for generalizable AI-generated video detection that leverages counterfactual intervention and causal disentanglement. It achieves over 90% overall accuracy on the challenging GenVidBench benchmark, with improvements of 0.194 in F1 and 0.104 in AUC over comparable state-of-the-art methods, while using only 10% of the training data. This work addresses a critical limitation of current AI-generated video detectors: their poor generalization to unseen generators. By improving cross-domain performance, G2VD could enhance the reliability of deepfake detection in real-world scenarios, where new generation methods constantly emerge. G2VD consists of a counterfactual intervention pipeline (CFIPipeline) that uses VAE-based reconstruction and frequency/pixel-domain alignment to reduce spurious correlations, and a causal disentanglement classifier with two domain-anchored branches and an HSIC-based constraint. The framework is validated on four public datasets, demonstrating consistent gains over baselines.

**Background**: AI-generated video detection is crucial for mitigating security risks from deepfakes. Existing detectors often rely on domain-specific biases, leading to performance drops on unseen generators. Counterfactual intervention and causal disentanglement are techniques from causal inference and representation learning that help models focus on intrinsic forensic cues rather than spurious correlations.
