## [VendorBench-100: Unified Benchmark for Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06254) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VendorBench-100 introduces a unified cross-paradigm benchmark for deepfake image detection, evaluating 36 models from commercial APIs, vision LLMs, and open-source detectors on a single adversarial 100-image corpus with a common evaluation framework. It ranks models primarily by Matthews correlation coefficient (MCC) and reveals that commercial APIs lead in median performance, while some strong rankers are miscalibrated at default thresholds. This benchmark addresses the lack of consistent evaluation across deepfake detection paradigms, enabling direct comparison and highlighting practical deployment issues like threshold miscalibration. It provides valuable insights for practitioners and researchers, emphasizing that no single metric is safe and that MCC and specificity should accompany ROC-AUC and accuracy. The benchmark includes a taxonomy of eight edge-case families, such as face swaps, text-to-video stills, and AI photo edits, to emphasize real-world difficulty. Across all 36 models, MCC and ROC-AUC are strongly correlated (Pearson r ~ 0.86), but a subset of models are miscalibrated at their shipped default threshold, meaning high ROC-AUC can overstate deployability. The authors release the complete evaluation framework and results on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Deepfake image detection is typically served by three paradigms: commercial APIs, zero-shot vision-language models (VLMs), and open-source detectors, which are rarely compared under a common protocol. The Matthews correlation coefficient (MCC) is a balanced measure for binary classification, accounting for all four confusion matrix categories, unlike accuracy or F1 which can be misleading on imbalanced datasets. This benchmark aims to provide a unified evaluation to facilitate fair comparison and practical deployment decisions.

## [Layout-Aware Framework for Cross-Version Differencing of Scientific Documents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14117) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a layout-aware, heterogeneous element-aware framework for cross-version differencing of scientific documents, which decomposes documents into typed elements and uses alignment-first and structure-aware reasoning to detect changes across text, tables, formulas, and figures. The framework achieves F1 scores of 0.903 for text, 0.855 for tables, 0.862 for formulas, and 0.845 for figures on real-world PDF data. This work addresses a critical gap in document processing by preserving layout and structural information that text-based methods lose, while offering semantic interpretability that image-based methods lack. It could significantly improve proofreading and version control workflows in scholarly publishing and technical documentation, benefiting editors, researchers, and NLP practitioners. The framework consists of three phases: layout parsing and element decomposition, cross-version heterogeneous element alignment, and type-aware difference comparison with document-level change aggregation. Ablation studies confirm the effectiveness of cross-version alignment, type-specific representations, structure-aware reasoning, and compatibility-weight design.

**Background**: Scientific documents are page-structured with heterogeneous elements like text, tables, formulas, and figures. Traditional differencing methods either flatten documents into text sequences, losing layout, or treat them as images, which is sensitive to rendering and lacks semantic understanding. Layout-aware document analysis has emerged to address these limitations by segmenting pages into typed regions and using vision-language models.

This paper introduces a partially-automated method to assess LLM security knowledge gaps using authoritative consumer protection information, demonstrated on identity theft and impostor scams across Gemini and GPT models. This method reduces the manual effort and expertise required for security benchmarking, enabling broader and more efficient evaluation of LLM security knowledge. ## [REGEN: Replay-Recycling Distillation for Efficient LLM Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19450) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces REGEN, a method that uses offline reinforcement learning to distill multiple specialized teacher policies into a single generalist LLM by recycling the teachers' replay memory, eliminating the need for on-policy rollout sampling. This approach matches the accuracy of multi-teacher on-policy distillation (MOPD) at substantially lower computational cost. REGEN addresses the scalability bottleneck of online RL and MOPD in LLM post-training by decoupling rollout sampling from backward training, potentially turning online RL into a data synthesis process. This could make large-scale post-training of generalist models more accessible and cost-effective for the AI community. REGEN is evaluated on mathematical reasoning, code generation, and instruction following, matching MOPD's accuracy. The method completely decouples rollout sampling from backward passes, and the code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Large-scale online reinforcement learning (RL) is commonly used to elicit advanced abilities in LLMs, but it is computationally expensive. Multi-teacher on-policy distillation (MOPD) is a technique that distills knowledge from multiple specialized teachers into a generalist model, but it still requires coupled inference and backward passes. Offline RL learns from a fixed dataset without additional environment interaction, and replay memory is a by-product of RL training that can be reused.

The method interprets value functions as Laplace transforms, allowing flexible combination of multiple horizons. Experiments in MiniGrid show that the approach identifies effective discount factors without manual tuning, even across task switches.

## [Linguistic Rules as Prompt Compressors Cut Inference Costs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25335) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a novel prompt compression method that uses deterministic linguistic rules instead of LM-based scoring, eliminating the need for LM forward passes during compression. The approach is evaluated across multiple datasets and achieves performance comparable to advanced compression strategies. This work could significantly reduce the computational cost of LLM inference by making prompt compression cheaper and faster, which is crucial for scaling LLM applications. It also bridges linguistic theory with practical LLM efficiency, potentially inspiring more interpretable and rule-based approaches in the field. The method uses offline evolutionary search over lexical, syntactic, semantic, and discourse seeds to find competitive rule combinations, requiring only CPU-side processing at deployment. Performance is strongest under light-to-moderate compression and degrades with more aggressive compression, with distinct patterns in Direct and Reconstruction paths.

Linguistic research has long studied cues for identifying informative content, which can be operationalized as deterministic rules. This paper explores whether such rules alone can serve as effective compressors, potentially avoiding the need for LM-based scoring.

<a id="item-268"></a>
## [I2VShield: Efficient Proactive Defense for DiT-based Image-to-Video Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25522) ⭐️ 7.0/10

I2VShield introduces a proactive defense framework for Diffusion Transformer (DiT)-based image-to-video (I2V) models, using a generative adversarial attack approach. It combines a text-adaptive perturbation generation framework with an untargeted Multimodal Attention Disruption (MAD) attack to reduce GPU memory requirements while maintaining visual imperceptibility. This work addresses the underexplored area of proactive defenses against I2V models, which are increasingly misused for generating deceptive videos. By reducing computational overhead compared to gradient-based methods, I2VShield makes proactive defense more accessible and practical, potentially enhancing AI security and content authenticity in the generative model ecosystem. The framework consists of two components: a text-adaptive perturbation generation framework integrating adversarial learning, and an untargeted Multimodal Attention Disruption (MAD) attack that maximizes deviation of internal attention features from clean states. Experiments show competitive protection performance across datasets and mainstream DiT-based I2V models, particularly in disrupting spatiotemporal coherence, with substantially reduced computational costs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion Transformer (DiT) is a class of diffusion models that replace the traditional U-Net backbone with a transformer architecture, operating on latent patches. Image-to-video (I2V) models generate videos from a single image, and their misuse raises concerns about deepfake content. Proactive defenses typically use gradient-based adversarial attacks, which require significant GPU memory; I2VShield aims to overcome this limitation.

## [LAIA Dataset: Human Attention Annotations for Interpretable End-to-End Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25570) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The LAIA dataset, a synthetic dataset with over 15 hours of driving data from 44 participants collected via the CARLA simulator, has been released. It includes synchronized eye-tracking data alongside RGB images, segmentation, depth, optical flow, and CAN bus signals to support interpretability in end-to-end autonomous driving. This dataset addresses a critical gap in end-to-end autonomous driving research by providing human attention data, which is essential for improving model interpretability and safety. It enables researchers to train attention-aware models and compare human attention with model attention, potentially accelerating the adoption of end-to-end systems. The dataset includes six weather conditions and carefully crafted scenarios to evoke natural responses. It supports applications such as training attention-aware AI drivers, predicting driver behavior, detecting anomalous attention patterns, and improving explainability.

## [BeyondUncertainty: LLM Confidence Guides Smarter Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25600) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces BeyondUncertainty, a method that uses verbalized confidence from black-box LLMs to decide whether to retrieve external information, achieving higher F1 scores while reducing retrieved passages by 20.4% compared to always retrieving. This approach addresses the inefficiency and noise of indiscriminate retrieval in RAG systems, offering a practical way to balance accuracy and computational cost. It could lead to more efficient and accurate question-answering systems, especially in resource-constrained environments. BeyondUncertainty uses a model-specific threshold selected on validation data, and low-confidence questions trigger top-5 TF-IDF retrieval followed by a second answer call. The method was evaluated on 27,000 policy instances across six QA benchmarks and three model families, achieving 0.483 mean token-level F1 versus 0.467 for always retrieval and 0.401 for no retrieval.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) enhances LLMs by retrieving relevant external information, but always retrieving can introduce irrelevant evidence and increase computation. Verbalized confidence is a technique where an LLM outputs a confidence score along with its answer, which can be used to gauge its certainty. TF-IDF is a classic information retrieval method that scores documents based on term frequency and inverse document frequency.

## [HYSET: Set-Level Tool Retrieval for LLM Agents via Hyperedge Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25718) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces HYSET, a set-level tool retrieval method for LLM agents that models tool co-invocation as a hypergraph and uses query-conditioned hyperedge prediction to evaluate tool sets jointly. It outperforms state-of-the-art baselines on ToolBench and supports zero-shot/few-shot transfer. Existing tool retrievers score tools in isolation or sequentially, ignoring the joint utility of a candidate set. HYSET addresses this gap by treating the tool set as the unit of scoring, which can improve agent performance in real-world tasks and offers a unified view of existing retrieval paradigms. HYSET captures size-dependent tool compatibility through cardinality-specific interactions and is designed as a pre-selection module requiring no modification to the downstream agent. Experiments on ToolBench show consistent improvements in both retrieval performance and end-to-end task success.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents often rely on external tools to complete tasks, but the tool library can contain thousands of tools, making it impossible to fit all in a prompt. Tool retrieval selects a small relevant subset before the agent acts. Hyperedge prediction is a task in hypergraph analysis that identifies missing or future higher-order interactions among sets of nodes.

## [PRISM-AH: A New Framework for Video-Level Ambivalence and Hesitancy Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25961) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces PRISM-AH, a framework that models ambivalence and hesitancy as multimodal conflict over time, using aligned frozen encoders and a streaming model. On a public test set of 525 videos, it achieves a macro F1 of 0.6133, significantly outperforming the zero-shot baseline of 0.2827. This work addresses a challenging problem in affective computing—recognizing ambivalence and hesitancy from video, which is crucial for understanding health behavior change. The framework's strong performance over the baseline suggests a promising direction for multimodal conflict modeling, potentially impacting fields like healthcare and human-computer interaction. PRISM-AH aligns frozen vision, audio, and text encoders into short time windows, then uses a lightweight streaming model to score cross-modal dissonance and predict next windows. It also incorporates dense window-level annotations, behavior prototypes, participant metadata, and a knowledge-guided LLM that fuses its verdict late only when validation performance improves.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Ambivalence and hesitancy are conflicting affective states that can delay or prevent health behavior change. Recognizing these states from video is difficult because signals arise from disagreements across facial, vocal, linguistic, and bodily modalities, and vary across individuals. Traditional approaches often rely on feature fusion, but PRISM-AH treats A/H as a multimodal conflict that unfolds over time, using frozen encoders and a streaming model to capture temporal dynamics.

## [The Easy Trap: LLMs Underestimate Misconception-Driven Difficulty](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26067) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This study evaluates LLM-generated difficulty ratings against empirical student performance on 32 arithmetic items, finding moderate rank correlations (Spearman's rho = 0.52-0.70) but systematic misestimation for fraction items, such as an item with only 34.16% correct for 100 : 1/2. This matters because LLMs are increasingly used to estimate item difficulty in educational assessment and adaptive systems; systematic underestimation of misconception-driven items could introduce bias in test design and personalized learning, potentially harming student assessment accuracy. The study used four LLM-based systems to generate difficulty ratings on a 1-100 scale across multiple runs (N = 640 ratings), compared with empirical difficulty from 770 Indonesian undergraduates using Classical Test Theory (CTT) and Item Response Theory (2PL). The authors propose the term 'Easy Trap' to describe the systematic underestimation of misconception-driven items.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Classical Test Theory (CTT) defines item difficulty as the proportion of correct responses (p-value), which is sample-dependent, while Item Response Theory (IRT) models difficulty as a parameter (b) on the ability scale, which is sample-independent. Fraction arithmetic is known to be challenging for students due to common misconceptions, such as not understanding the need to find a common denominator before adding or subtracting fractions.

The proposed dataset includes diverse ethnicities, age groups, genders, poses (frontal, back, full/half body, side, mirror selfies), and backgrounds, with possible artifacts hiding faces. ## [Hybrid Deep Learning Quantifies Dermal Exposure from Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26170) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This study introduces a hybrid computer vision method combining Mask R-CNN and color-based segmentation to quantify exposed skin from 170 indoor-painting images, achieving approximately 80% agreement with human estimates for dermal exposure assessment. This approach offers a scalable, semi-quantitative way to extract dermal exposure information from images, potentially enhancing safety assessments in occupational and environmental health settings. It could reduce reliance on manual estimation and enable broader, more consistent exposure monitoring. The method first uses Mask R-CNN to identify human subjects and remove background interference, then applies a color-based algorithm to segment exposed skin. The resulting exposed-skin-to-body pixel ratios showed about 80% agreement with human estimates, with future extensions planned for body-part recognition, PPE detection, and video-based analysis.

## [Fine-Tuned 300M Encoder Beats LLMs in Financial Inconsistency Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26368) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper benchmarks fine-grained inconsistency classification on the SBID-FD financial disclosure dataset, comparing various models. A fine-tuned 300M encoder achieved 61.9% accuracy, slightly outperforming a LoRA-adapted Qwen3.5-9B (61.5%) and GPT-5.4 (61.3%). This finding suggests that compact supervised encoders can be more efficient than large generative models for specialized financial NLP tasks, potentially reducing computational costs. It also highlights the importance of evidence localization for improving inconsistency classification, guiding future research directions. The study uses a fixed 5,940-instance snapshot of SBID-FD with 11 inconsistency labels and paired reference evidence spans. Supplying gold evidence spans improves the fine-tuned encoder to 65.3%, while automatically predicted spans recover only part of that gain, indicating localization quality is a bottleneck. Class-level analysis shows Referential inconsistencies are sensitive to localization, while Factual and Logical inconsistencies remain difficult even with evidence.

## [Audio-Anchored Fusion of DiT Residuals for Cross-Domain Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26472) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes an audio-anchored fusion method that uses multi-ratio reconstruction residuals from a frozen Diffusion Transformer (DiT) for cross-domain audio deepfake detection, achieving competitive EER on ASVspoof 5 and ITW benchmarks. This work addresses the critical problem of domain shift in audio deepfake detection, where models often degrade on unseen generators or recording conditions. The proposed method offers a novel way to leverage reconstruction residuals as complementary evidence, potentially improving robustness in real-world applications. The method uses a frozen DiT trained only on bona fide speech, with masking ratios 0.5, 0.75, and 0.9 to generate residual maps. The audio-anchored detector passes projected frozen-WavLM auditory representations into the fusion sum without gate-based attenuation, using residuals only as a scalar-gated additive correction. The seed-42 run achieves 6.5442% EER on ASVspoof 5 Eval and 13.8372% on ITW Full; three-seed means are 6.8885% and 15.3328% respectively.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Audio deepfake detection aims to distinguish synthetic or manipulated speech from bona fide speech. Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) are generative models that can reconstruct input audio; the reconstruction error (residual) can indicate whether the audio is genuine. WavLM is a self-supervised speech representation model that captures both content and speaker information. ASVspoof is a series of challenges providing benchmark datasets for spoofing and deepfake detection.

This paper introduces an attention-based deep learning framework that treats brain regions as tokens and uses a Transformer-inspired self-attention mechanism to classify Alzheimer's disease from resting-state fMRI connectivity matrices. The model achieves 88.95% accuracy and a ROC-AUC of 0.90 on a longitudinal ADNI cohort, outperforming traditional handcrafted feature approaches. It could improve early diagnosis and contribute to the growing trend of applying transformer-based models to medical imaging. The framework uses a subject-wise evaluation protocol to prevent information leakage across visits and incorporates class-weighted optimization to handle mild class imbalance. The model operates directly on functional connectivity matrices without manual feature engineering, and the study uses data from the Alzheimer's Disease Neuroimaging Initiative (ADNI).

Attention mechanisms, originally from transformers, allow models to weigh the importance of different input parts, making them suitable for capturing global dependencies in brain networks.

## [CoCaRS: Correlation Calibration for Heterogeneous Knowledge Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27054) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces CoCaRS, a novel method for heterogeneous knowledge distillation that calibrates feature decorrelation through Confusion Evidence Estimation (CEE) and Strength Allocation Control (SAC), and adaptively regulates the redundancy suppression objective via Adaptive Coefficient Regulation (ACR). Experiments on CIFAR-100 and ImageNet-1K show improved distillation performance and reduced sensitivity to coefficient settings. This work addresses a key challenge in heterogeneous knowledge distillation, where architectural differences between teacher and student models hinder effective knowledge transfer. By improving redundancy suppression while preserving structural information, CoCaRS could enhance the performance of compact student models across diverse architectures, benefiting model compression and deployment in resource-constrained environments. CoCaRS consists of three main components: CEE captures reliable semantic relations for correlation estimation, SAC preserves discriminative structure during decorrelation, and ACR adjusts the contribution of the redundancy suppression loss based on its relative scale. The method is validated on CIFAR-100 and ImageNet-1K, and the code is planned to be released.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge distillation (KD) is a model compression technique where a compact student model learns from a powerful teacher model. Heterogeneous KD extends this to cases where teacher and student have different architectures, which introduces representation discrepancies. Redundancy suppression, as in the RSD method, reduces architecture-exclusive information by decorrelating features, but may weaken structural information. CoCaRS builds on this by calibrating the decorrelation process to better preserve useful structure.

## [Event-Conditioned Credibility for Decision-Oriented Evidence Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.04128) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces event-conditioned credibility, a new metric that characterizes the relative credibility of evidence under different candidate-event hypotheses, and proposes a decision-oriented joint optimization model that couples credibility calculation, evidence fusion, and event decision. The model is solved via fixed-point iteration and Kuhn simplicial search, and numerical experiments show it outperforms representative credible evidence fusion methods. This work addresses a critical limitation in existing evidence fusion methods that may underestimate evidence deviating from the majority, which could be critical for correct decisions. By providing a decision-oriented framework, it has the potential to improve decision-making in heterogeneous and anomaly-prone information fusion scenarios, benefiting fields such as sensor fusion, multi-source intelligence, and automated decision systems. The proposed model is expressed as a continuous self-mapping on the probability simplex, with a direct fixed-point iteration as the default fast solver and a Kuhn simplicial search providing a mesh-dependent approximate fixed point if the direct iteration forms a periodic orbit. The method also introduces a plausibility-belief arithmetic-geometric divergence to calculate event-conditioned credibility, and Monte Carlo tests demonstrate a high empirical fixed-point attainment rate.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Evidence fusion combines information from multiple sources to support decision-making, but sources may have unequal precision and anomalies. Existing credible evidence fusion methods assess credibility through inter-evidence comparisons, which can underestimate evidence that deviates from the majority but is actually critical. Fixed-point iteration is a standard numerical method for solving equations of the form x = g(x), and Kuhn simplicial search is a combinatorial algorithm for finding approximate fixed points on triangulated domains.

## [Workflow Quantifies Downsampling Effects on Needle EMG Signals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.10191) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces a generalizable workflow that combines shape-based distortion metrics with classification performance to systematically evaluate information loss from downsampling in needle electromyography (nEMG) signals. The workflow was validated on a three-class neuromuscular disease classification task, demonstrating that shape-aware downsampling algorithms preserve diagnostic information better than standard decimation. This work addresses a critical computational bottleneck in near real-time nEMG analysis, which is emerging as a tool for detecting neuromuscular diseases. By providing a method to balance data reduction with model performance, it could accelerate clinical adoption and also serve as a template for other high-frequency time-series applications. The workflow uses shape-based distortion metrics and feature space analysis, alongside classification outcomes from feature-based machine learning models. The study found that shape-aware downsampling algorithms outperform standard decimation in preserving peak structure and overall signal morphology, while substantially reducing computational load.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Needle electromyography (nEMG) records electrical signals from muscle fibers to characterize neuromuscular disorders. These signals often have high and heterogeneous sampling rates, posing computational challenges for machine-learning models, especially in near real-time scenarios. Downsampling reduces data volume but can lose diagnostic information, so a systematic evaluation method is needed.

## [Agentic Reflection and Memory in LLM Extraction: A Controlled Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15715) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This arXiv preprint (2607.15715v2) empirically compares fixed LLM workflows with reflective agent variants for conference-paper dataset extraction, introducing an optimized agent condition (S2) with richer PDF tools and dynamic tool selection. The study emphasizes process-level behaviors such as tool execution, retries, reflection, memory use, runtime, and failure recovery. This work addresses a critical question in the AI community: whether agentic components like reflection and memory provide tangible benefits over simpler fixed workflows in information extraction. The findings could guide practitioners in designing more effective and controllable LLM-based extraction systems, potentially impacting fields that rely on structured data extraction from documents. The study uses conference-paper dataset extraction as a testbed, where systems must identify datasets mentioned in scholarly PDFs and produce structured records. Evaluation treats extraction coverage and field completeness as secondary outcomes, while focusing on process-level behavior. The optimized agent condition (S2) extends the task with richer PDF tools and dynamic tool selection, and the paper characterizes when agentic mechanisms change behavior and whether these changes improve task completion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Large language model (LLM) agents are increasingly used for complex tasks like information extraction, where they can leverage tools, reflection, and memory to improve performance. However, it is unclear whether these agentic components provide observable and controllable improvements over fixed workflows. This study aims to fill that gap by systematically comparing different agent designs in a controlled extraction task.

## [AI LEGO: Scaffolding Cross-Functional Responsible AI Collaboration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10300) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces AI LEGO, a web-based prototype that operationalizes boundary object theory to support cross-functional AI practitioners in early design stages. In a study with 18 practitioners, AI LEGO increased the volume and likelihood of harms identified compared to baseline worksheets. This work addresses a persistent coordination challenge in responsible AI, where technical and non-technical roles struggle to collaborate effectively on harm identification. By providing a shared visual structure and LLM-driven persona simulations, AI LEGO makes harm identification more accessible and fosters clearer collaborative RAI practices, which could improve the adoption of responsible AI in industry. The prototype uses interactive blocks for technical roles to draft development plans, while non-technical roles engage through stage-specific checklists and LLM-driven persona simulations. The study involved 18 cross-functional practitioners and found that AI LEGO increased the volume and likelihood of harms identified compared to baseline worksheets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Responsible AI (RAI) emphasizes addressing potential harms early in the AI development lifecycle through social-technical lenses. Cross-functional teams, which include members from different departments, often face coordination challenges in RAI practices. Boundary object theory suggests that shared artifacts can facilitate collaboration across different communities of practice. AI LEGO applies this theory to create a shared visual structure for harm identification.

<a id="item-285"></a>
## [PatchDenoiser: Lightweight Multi-Scale Denoiser for Low-Dose CT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.21987) ⭐️ 6.0/10

PatchDenoiser introduces a parameter-efficient, multi-scale patch-based denoising framework for low-dose CT imaging, decomposing denoising into local texture extraction and global context aggregation with spatially aware patch fusion. It achieves state-of-the-art PSNR and SSIM on the 2016 Mayo Low-Dose CT dataset while reducing parameters by ~9x and energy consumption per inference by ~27x compared to conventional CNN denoisers. This work addresses a clinically relevant problem by providing a practical, scalable solution for low-dose CT denoising that balances performance and computational efficiency. It could enable broader clinical deployment of deep learning denoisers, especially in resource-constrained settings, and improve image quality in cancer screening and pediatric imaging. PatchDenoiser is robust to variations in slice thickness, reconstruction kernels, and HU windows, and generalizes across scanners without fine-tuning. It outperforms state-of-the-art CNN- and GAN-based methods on the Mayo dataset, despite being ultra-lightweight with far fewer parameters and lower computational complexity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Low-dose CT reduces radiation exposure but introduces noise that degrades image quality. Traditional filtering methods over-smooth, while deep learning methods like CNNs, GANs, and transformers often require large models. PatchDenoiser uses a multi-scale patch-based approach to balance noise suppression and detail preservation, making it more practical for clinical use.

## [CVA: Lightweight Module for Efficient Micro-Video Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08810) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Compressed Video Aggregator (CVA), a lightweight module that compresses frozen VFM frame embeddings into a single video embedding via masked mean pooling, latent projection, and residual self-attention, achieving consistent performance gains with orders-of-magnitude reductions in training time and GPU memory on MicroLens and Short-Video benchmarks. This work addresses the efficiency bottleneck in micro-video recommendation by decoupling video information from preference learning, enabling scalable deployment on resource-constrained platforms. It also demonstrates that re-selecting key frames based on titles can boost performance across all methods, offering a practical data-centric improvement. CVA uses masked mean pooling to summarize frozen VFM frame embeddings into a semantic-consensus anchor, then projects it into a compact latent space and refines with residual self-attention and feedforward blocks. The authors also re-selected key frames using CLIP and titles, which further improved performance but may be sensitive to erroneous titles.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Micro-video recommendation relies on understanding video content, often using pre-trained vision foundation models (VFMs) to extract frame embeddings. However, processing many frames is computationally expensive, and existing methods typically integrate video features directly into preference models, increasing complexity. CVA proposes a decoupled, lightweight aggregation module that compresses video information into a single embedding, reducing overhead while preserving semantic content.

## [Human-Centered AI Framework Proposed for Lexicography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11808) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new paper proposes a human-centered AI (HCAI) framework for AI-assisted lexicography, identifying four dimensions: the augmented lexicographer, sociotechnical context, bias, and tool design. It argues that AI should augment rather than replace lexicographers, preserving professional agency and mitigating biases. This framework addresses critical concerns about the role of lexicographers and linguistic diversity in the age of generative AI. It provides a foundation for future research and responsible AI integration in lexicography, potentially influencing how AI tools are designed and deployed in language professions. The framework draws on HCAI principles and previous applications in other language professions, emphasizing meaningful human control and tool design centered on lexicographers' needs. The paper is available on arXiv (2607.11808v3) and was announced as a replace-cross update.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: Human-centered AI (HCAI) prioritizes human needs, values, and capabilities in AI design. Lexicography, the practice of dictionary-making, is increasingly using AI tools like Sketch Engine and Lexonomy, but generative AI raises concerns about job displacement and bias. The paper proposes a framework to guide AI integration in this field.

## [Unofficial FastLAS Tutorial: A Programmer's Guide](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23557) ⭐️ 6.0/10

An unofficial programmer's guide to FastLAS 2.2.0 has been published on arXiv, offering a hands-on tutorial with worked examples for Inductive Logic Programming (ILP). The tutorial covers syntax, examples of increasing difficulty, and highlights differences between FastLAS and ILASP, as well as between the --opl and --nopl algorithms. This tutorial lowers the barrier for practitioners to use FastLAS, a scalable ILP system, by providing practical, runnable examples. It clarifies key differences with ILASP and between learning algorithms, which can help users choose the right tool and configuration for their tasks. The tutorial is based on FastLAS 2.2.0 and includes set-off notes that flag differences from ILASP and between the --opl and --nopl algorithms. It is intended as an unofficial tutorial, not an official language specification, and keeps theory to a minimum.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 31, 04:00

**Background**: FastLAS is a system for Inductive Logic Programming (ILP), a subfield of symbolic AI that uses logic programming to represent examples, background knowledge, and hypotheses. It is based on the Context-dependent Learning from Answer Sets framework used by ILASP, and was originally presented at AAAI 2020. ILP systems like FastLAS search for logic program rules that explain given examples, which is useful for tasks requiring interpretable models.
